FILE: README.md
================================================
# JIG

**A small pipeline that turns a question into a typed decision program, resolves each
judgment against bounded sources, and returns a verdict with citations, the predicates it
resolved, and the ones it could not.** The point of the exercise is the second question:
can the *structure* of a decision be reused on the next question, with fresh facts, for a
fraction of the cost of working it out again?

```
python3 -m jig.demo                # the whole argument, ~1 s, in a terminal
python3 -m jig.server 8010         # http://localhost:8010  (live UI, replayable)
python3 -m unittest discover -s tests   # 68 tests
```

The UI is `web/app.html` — a single file with no external resources. Opened directly it
falls back to a snapshot baked from a real run (`tools/bake_preview.py`); served by
`jig.server` it executes everything live, including admitting a proposal.

---

## The claim under test

> A $0.18 frontier call that researches a question end to end is doing two things:
> finding facts, and deciding what the facts mean. Only the first one is new each time.
> The second one should be buildable once, verified, and reused.

Four things follow, and each has to be demonstrated rather than asserted:

1. **Programs are reusable.** Build cost is paid once per program and reported separately
   from the per-run cost, which must stay low on questions the author never saw.
2. **Retrieval should be directed by what is still unresolved.** Search for the predicate
   that still separates the answers, including evidence *against* the leading answer, and
   stop when it is resolved.
3. **The frontier model has a second job between runs**: proposing the predicate, exception
   or source requirement that the small path was missing — verified before adoption.
4. **Agreement is the weakest evidence available.** The real test is whether a decision
   moves when the world moves under it, and refuses when the evidence is pulled away.

## What it does, concretely

A question arrives:

> *Can we approve a $4,200 hosting purchase from Nimbus Hosting on 2026-11-05, charged to ENG-TOOLS?*

It is routed to a program and bound to its slots (`amount`, `vendor`, `category`,
`budget_line`, `as_of`). The program says what the answer depends on:

```
PROC-01 @ 2.0   choose: P6 OR (P1 AND P2 AND (NOT P3 OR P4) AND P5)
  P1  within the manager limit in force on the purchase date     policy/          exact
  P2  vendor approved in the vendor register                     registry/vendors exact
  P3  does this category process personal data?                  data taxonomy    exact
  P4  is the vendor's security review current (12 months)?       registry/security exact
  P5  does the budget line have enough remaining and is it open? registry/budget  exact
  P6  does an exception pre-approve this request?                memos + calendar judge
  → APPROVE / DENY / DEFER (unresolved decisive predicate) / ESCALATE (out of scope)
```

Resolution is gap-directed and stops early:

- the limit fails → the vendor, security and budget predicates **cannot** change the
  outcome, so nothing is retrieved for them (visible in the trace, not just claimed);
- the budget is frozen but a memo pre-approves conference travel and the calendar lists
  the event → two documents, one judgment, `APPROVE`;
- the vendor has no register entry → `DEFER` plus the specific open question, instead of a
  guess;
- nothing in the library covers office leases → `ESCALATE`, which costs one frontier call
  and is counted as one.

## Results

Every number below is produced by executing the pipeline over the 15 labelled questions in
`jig/programs.py`. Arm A is the exception and is labelled as such everywhere.

| arm | correct | material errors | overreach | defer | escalate | citations | cost / decision | latency | predicates |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** frontier, end to end *(modelled)* | 15/15 | 0 | 0 | — | — | n/a | $0.1785 | 92 s | — |
| **B1** single answer + retrieval | 9/15 | 4 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 14% | $0.000415 | 3.5 s | 1.0 |
| **B2** decomposition + retrieval | 15/15 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 86% | $0.000495 | 3.9 s | 4.1 |
| **B3** reused program + targeted retrieval | 15/15 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 86% | $0.000404 | 3.3 s | 3.2 |

*Overreach* = a binary answer where the correct response was `DEFER`/`ESCALATE`. *Citations* =
the decisive document appears in the decision's citations. B2 and B3 reach the same verdicts;
the difference is work done, which is what the predicate column measures.

**Economics.** One-time build: **$0.8205** across 3 programs / 4 versions. Per decision:
**$0.000404**, i.e. **442×** cheaper than the frontier arm. Break-even at **4.61 questions**.
On the 10 held-out questions the programs were never authored against: **$0.000396** each,
**450×**. Latency: 3.3 s vs 92 s, **28×**. Escalations: 1 of 15 (6.7%), each costing one
frontier call.

**Why a library version was rejected, and what admitted the next one.** PROC-01 v1.0 gets 8
of 9 labelled procurement questions right — and approves the Nimbus purchase, whose vendor's
security review expired in 2024. It passes 3 of 5 interventions:

| intervention | expected | v1.0 | v2.0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| suspend the vendor | DENY | DENY ✓ | DENY ✓ |
| add irrelevant wording | same verdict *and* citations | citations moved ✗ | unchanged ✓ |
| delete the security review register | DEFER | **APPROVE** ✗ | DEFER ✓ |
| ask it before v2.0 took effect | follow the policy in force | DENY ✓ | DENY ✓ |
| ask about an office lease | ESCALATE | ESCALATE ✓ | ESCALATE ✓ |

The third row is the whole argument. **A program with no predicate for a source cannot
notice the source is gone.** Agreement-based evaluation cannot see this: v1.0 agrees with
the frontier on that question. So a proposal is written (add the personal-data and
security-review conditions, per policy v2.0 §5), a candidate is built, and it is admitted
**only** after passing 5/5 interventions and 9/9 labelled without regressing.

**Reuse under fresh facts.** Five variants, each a corpus edit or a date change, re-run
through the same program versions — 5/5 behave as required, no rebuild, per-decision cost
unchanged:

| variant | what moved |
|---|---|
| budget topped up, manager limit raised to $7,500 | q03 DENY → APPROVE |
| Nimbus passes its security review | q02 DENY → APPROVE |
| conference pre-approval memo withdrawn | q05 APPROVE → DENY |
| Orbit's addendum lapses | q11 CLEARED → BLOCKED |
| ask everything as of 2026-05-01, before v2.0 | q01 APPROVE → DENY, and nothing else |

The last one is the case a cached answer gets wrong: no document changed, only the date, and
the decision follows the policy version that was in force.

## What is measured and what is modelled

- **Executed:** every verdict, every predicate evaluation, every skip, every gap, every
  intervention, every citation, every call count. The cost arithmetic is auditable from
  `core.RATES` and the call counts.
- **Modelled:** the *prices and latencies* in `core.RATES` (declared token rates, not
  measured invoices), and **arm A wholesale** — its answers are the labelled ground truth
  standing in for a real frontier run. Labelled as modelled in the UI wherever it appears.
- **Offline:** judge predicates run on the reference evaluator (entailment patterns over the
  retrieved excerpts, deterministic). `jig.judge.LLMJudge` is the same interface wired to an
  OpenAI-compatible endpoint via `JIG_LLM_BASE` / `JIG_LLM_KEY` / `JIG_LLM_MODEL`; no model
  was called for these results.
- **Synthetic:** the 14-document corpus. It is a fixture with deliberate structure — two
  policy versions, a superseded rule, a suspended vendor, an expired review, a frozen budget
  line, an exception memo — not anyone's real policy.
- **Error budget:** exact over 15 questions. No sampling, so no confidence intervals, and
  far too few questions to estimate an error rate. Each ground truth is derivable from the
  documents in `corpus.py` and auditable in `programs.py`; the test suite pins them.

## Files

```
jig/core.py        3-valued logic, program/predicate/decision types, cost model
jig/corpus.py      frozen dated corpus, version resolution, scoped retrieval
jig/judge.py       exact operators (code) + judge operators (reference + model adapter)
jig/engine.py      planner, slot binding, gap-directed loop, early stop, combining
jig/programs.py    the three programs, the proposal, the labelled set, the library
jig/verify.py      the intervention suite, verification, admission
jig/bench.py       four arms, canonical scoring, failure localisation, economics
jig/reuse.py       savings curve, fact-change variants, held-out measurement
jig/server.py      HTTP API (stdlib only)
jig/demo.py        the narrated terminal run
web/app.html       single-file UI, no external resources
tools/bake_preview.py  bakes a live run into the UI for offline preview
tests/test_jig.py  68 tests
docs/DESIGN.md     requirement → implementation map
```

## Known limitations

- **Fifteen questions is a fixture, not a benchmark.** Enough to make the mechanism legible
  and falsifiable, not enough to claim an error rate.
- **Registers are read as current state.** Versioned policies are resolved by the date in
  force; a register or memo is a point-in-time record whose last-updated date is shown with
  the citation rather than used to suppress it for earlier questions. Snapshotted registers
  would be needed in production.
- **The judge is not a model here.** The program structure is what is under test, and it does
  not change when a model is plugged in — but the judge's *accuracy* is untested by this
  work.
- **Scope is deliberately narrow.** Three programs, one domain, one corpus. The claim is about
  the mechanism (programs, gap-directed retrieval, verification by intervention), not about
  coverage.

let LIVE = false;
let LAST_Q = null;   // sessionStorage is unavailable in sandboxed previews
const CACHE = {};

/* ---------- data ---------- */
async function get(path){
  if (CACHE[path]) return CACHE[path];
  if (LIVE) {
    try {
      const r = await fetch(path, {headers:{'accept':'application/json'}});
      if (!r.ok) throw new Error(r.status);
      const j = await r.json(); CACHE[path] = j; return j;
    } catch(e){ /* fall through to snapshot */ }
  }
  const s = SNAPSHOT && SNAPSHOT[path];
  if (s === undefined) throw new Error('no snapshot for '+path);
  CACHE[path] = s; return s;
}
async function post(path, body){
  const r = await fetch(path, {method:'POST', headers:{'content-type':'application/json'},
                              body: JSON.stringify(body||{})});
  if (!r.ok) throw new Error((await r.json()).error || r.status);
  CACHE && Object.keys(CACHE).forEach(k=>delete CACHE[k]);
  return r.json();
}

/* ---------- helpers ---------- */
const el = (h) => { const t = document.createElement('template'); t.innerHTML = h.trim(); return t.content.firstElementChild; };
const esc = (s) => String(s ?? '').replace(/[&<>"']/g, c => ({'&':'&amp;','<':'&lt;','>':'&gt;','"':'&quot;',"'":'&#39;'}[c]));
const money = (n, d=6) => '$' + Number(n||0).toFixed(d);
const money4 = (n) => '$' + Number(n||0).toFixed(4);
const clamp = (s, n) => { s = String(s||''); return s.length > n ? s.slice(0, n-1) + '…' : s; };
const canonChip = (c) => {
  const map = {YES:'yes', NO:'no', DEFER:'unk', ESCALATE:'esc'};
  return `<span class="chip ${map[c]||''}">${esc(c)}</span>`;
};
const verdictClass = (v) => ({YES:'v-yes', NO:'v-no', DEFER:'v-defer', ESCALATE:'v-esc'}[v] || 'v-defer');
const ms = (n) => (Number(n||0) >= 1000 ? (Number(n)/1000).toFixed(1)+' s' : Math.round(n)+' ms');

const PRESETS = [
  ['q01','$4,200 analytics · Acme · budget open','Can we approve a $4,200 analytics subscription from Acme Analytics on 2026-11-05, charged to ENG-TOOLS?'],
  ['q02','$4,200 hosting · Nimbus · review expired','Can we approve a $4,200 hosting purchase from Nimbus Hosting on 2026-11-05, charged to ENG-TOOLS?'],
  ['q05','$3,800 conference travel · frozen budget','Can we approve $3,800 of conference travel for J. Reyes to attend DataOps Summit 2026 on 2026-11-05, charged to MKT-EVENTS?'],
  ['q07','same as q01, dated before v2.0','Can we approve a $4,200 analytics subscription from Acme Analytics on 2026-05-01, charged to ENG-TOOLS?'],
  ['q09','unknown vendor · must defer','Can we approve a $900 support plan from Halcyon Data on 2026-11-05, charged to ENG-TOOLS?'],
  ['q10','office lease · out of scope','Should we renew the office lease for our Boston office in 2027?'],
  ['q12','hardware onboarding · early stop','Can we onboard Kestrel Hardware for hardware supply on 2026-11-05?'],
  ['q14','emergency equipment · CFO notified','Can we proceed without prior approval on an emergency purchase of $9,000 of ventilation safety equipment on 2026-11-05, CFO notified within 24 hours: yes?'],
];

/* ---------- views ---------- */
const VIEWS = {};

VIEWS.decide = async () => {
  let state = null;
  try { state = await get('/api/state'); } catch(e) {}
  const question = LAST_Q || PRESETS[1][2];
  const main = document.getElementById('main');
  main.innerHTML = `
    <h2>One question, traced end to end</h2>
    <p class="lede">The question is translated into a <b>typed decision program</b> — the predicates it
    depends on, the sources each may read, what happens if a source is missing, and how the answers
    combine. Predicates that cannot change the outcome are not retrieved for. The result is a verdict,
    its citations, and the open questions that remain.</p>
    <div class="card">
      <div class="row" style="gap:8px;margin-bottom:10px" id="presets"></div>
      <textarea id="q">${esc(question)}</textarea>
      <div class="row" style="margin-top:10px">
        <button class="act" id="run">Decide</button>
        <span class="small faint" id="mode"></span>
      </div>
    </div>
    <div id="out" style="margin-top:16px"></div>`;

  const box = main.querySelector('#presets');
  PRESETS.forEach(([id, label, text]) => {
    const b = el(`<button class="preset" title="${esc(text)}">${esc(label)}</button>`);
    b.onclick = () => { main.querySelector('#q').value = text; run(); };
    box.appendChild(b);
  });
  main.querySelector('#mode').textContent = LIVE ? 'live: every run is executed now'
    : 'static snapshot: results below were produced by the running pipeline and baked into this file';
  async function run(){
    const q = main.querySelector('#q').value.trim();
    if (!q) return;
    LAST_Q = q;
    const out = main.querySelector('#out');
    out.innerHTML = '<div class="empty">resolving…</div>';
    let d;
    if (LIVE) {
      try { d = (await post('/api/decide', {question:q})).decision; }
      catch(e){ out.innerHTML = `<div class="banner">${esc(e.message)}</div>`; return; }
    } else {
      const snap = await get('/api/decide:' + q);
      d = snap.decision;
    }
    out.innerHTML = '';
    out.appendChild(renderDecision(d, state));
  }
  main.querySelector('#run').onclick = run;
  if (LIVE) run(); else {
    try { const presets = await get('/api/decide:presets'); } catch(e){}
    run();
  }
};

function renderDecision(d, state){
  const prog = (state && state.library.find(l => l.program.id === d.program_id)) || null;
  const v = d.verdict;
  const wrap = el('<div class="grid"></div>');

  // verdict
  const vc = el(`<div class="verdict ${verdictClass(v)}">
    <div>
      <div class="vlabel">${esc(d.label)}</div>
      <div class="small dim" style="margin-top:6px">${esc(d.question)}</div>
      <div class="small faint" style="margin-top:8px">
        ${d.stopped_early ? 'stopped as soon as the rule was decided' : 'all relevant predicates resolved'}
        · ${d.evaluated} predicate${d.evaluated===1?'':'s'} evaluated
        · ${d.skipped.length} skipped
      </div>
    </div>
    <div class="kv">
      <span>program</span><span>${esc(d.program_id||'—')} @ ${esc(d.program_version||'—')}</span>
      <span>as of</span><span>${esc(d.as_of)}</span>
      <span>cost</span><span>${money(d.cost.total)}</span>
      <span>latency</span><span>${ms(d.latency_ms)}</span>
      <span>judge calls</span><span>${d.cost.judge_calls||0}</span>
      <span>frontier call</span><span class="faint">${d.cost.escalated ? 'used' : 'not used'}</span>
    </div>
  </div>`);
  wrap.appendChild(vc);

  if (d.gaps && d.gaps.length){
    const g = el('<div class="card"></div>');
    g.innerHTML = `<h3>Open questions</h3>` + d.gaps.map(x =>
      `<div class="small" style="color:var(--defer)">— ${esc(x)}</div>`).join('');
    wrap.appendChild(g);
  }

  // ledger
  const led = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  led.innerHTML = `<div class="spread"><h3>Predicate ledger</h3>
      <span class="small faint">${esc(prog ? prog.program.combine : '')}</span></div>`;
  const t = el('<table><thead><tr><th>#</th><th>Judgment</th><th>Answer</th><th>By</th><th>Rationale and sources</th></tr></thead><tbody></tbody></table>');
  const tb = t.querySelector('tbody');
  (d.findings||[]).forEach(f => {
    const pred = prog && prog.program.predicates.find(p => p.id === f.predicate);
    const ans = f.answer === 'yes' ? 'yes' : f.answer === 'no' ? 'no' : (f.answer === 'n/a' ? 'n/a' : 'unknown');
    const chipCls = ans === 'yes' ? 'yes' : ans === 'no' ? 'no' : (ans === 'n/a' ? '' : 'unk');
    const cites = (f.citations||[]).map(c =>
      `<details><summary>${esc(c.doc_id)} <span class="faint">· ${esc(c.date||'')}</span></summary>
         <blockquote>${esc(clamp(c.quote || '', 320))}</blockquote></details>`).join('');
    const tr = el(`<tr>
      <td class="mono" style="color:var(--faint)">${esc(f.predicate)}</td>
      <td>${esc(pred ? pred.ask : f.predicate)}
        <div class="small faint mono" style="margin-top:3px">${esc(pred ? 'reads: ' + (pred.requires.join(', ') || 'request only') : '')}
        ${pred && pred.on_missing && pred.on_missing!=='unknown' ? ' · if missing: ' + esc(pred.on_missing) : ''}</div></td>
      <td><span class="chip ${chipCls}">${esc(ans)}</span>
        ${f.answer==='unknown' && f.gap ? `<div class="small" style="color:var(--defer);margin-top:4px">needs: ${esc(clamp(f.gap,90))}</div>`:''}</td>
      <td><span class="tag ${f.evaluator==='judge'?'judge':'code'}">${esc(f.evaluator)}</span></td>
      <td>${esc(f.rationale)}${cites}</td></tr>`);
    tb.appendChild(tr);
  });
  led.appendChild(t);
  wrap.appendChild(led);

  // skipped
  if (d.skipped && d.skipped.length){
    const sk = el('<div class="card"></div>');
    sk.innerHTML = `<h3>Not looked for</h3>
      <div class="small dim" style="margin-bottom:8px">Predicates that could not change the outcome
      given what was already resolved. Nothing was retrieved for them.</div>` +
      d.skipped.map(s => `<div class="small mono" style="color:var(--faint)">${esc(s.predicate)} — ${esc(s.reason)}</div>`).join('');
    wrap.appendChild(sk);
  }

  // trace
  const tr = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  const steps = (d.trace||[]).filter(s => s.step === 'resolve');
  tr.innerHTML = `<h3>What it read</h3>
    <div class="small dim" style="margin-bottom:8px">Each predicate searches only inside its declared
    source scope. A predicate whose scope contains nothing relevant reports a gap instead of widening
    the search.</div>` +
    steps.map(s => `<div style="margin-bottom:10px">
      <div class="mono small">${esc(s.predicate)} · scope ${esc((s.scope||[]).join(' ') || 'request only')}</div>
      <div class="small faint">query: ${esc(clamp(s.query, 150))}</div>
      <div class="small faint">returned: ${(s.hits||[]).map(h=>esc(h.doc_id)).join(', ') || '<i>nothing</i>'}</div>
    </div>`).join('');
  wrap.appendChild(tr);
  return wrap;
}

VIEWS.programs = async () => {
  let lib = null, verify = null;
  try { lib = await get('/api/library'); } catch(e){}
  try { verify = await get('/api/verify'); } catch(e){}
  const main = document.getElementById('main');
  main.innerHTML = `<div class="spread"><h2>The library</h2>
      <button class="ghost" id="reset">Reset library</button></div>
    <p class="lede">A program is the reusable object: predicates, source scope per predicate,
    missing-knowledge policy, combining rule, and the interventions it had to survive to be admitted.
    Versions are additive — a proposal creates a candidate, verification admits or rejects it.</p>
    <div id="lib"></div>`;
  const host = main.querySelector('#lib');
  const reset = main.querySelector('#reset');
  if (reset) reset.onclick = async () => {
    if (!LIVE) { toast('Static snapshot. Run <span class="mono">python3 -m jig.server</span> to replay from v1.0.'); return; }
    await post('/api/reset', {});
    toast('Library reset to PROC-01 v1.0 — unverified. Open a proposal to rebuild and admit v2.0.', 'ok');
    setTimeout(() => VIEWS.programs(), 900);
  };
  if (!lib) { host.innerHTML = '<div class="empty">no library data</div>'; return; }

  const proof = {};
  ((verify && verify.before_after) ? Object.entries(verify.before_after) : []).forEach(([k,v]) => proof[k]=v);

  lib.library.forEach(entry => {
    const p = entry.program;
    const ver = p.verification || {};
    const badge = p.verified
      ? `<span class="tag pass">verified ${ver.interventions_passed ?? '?'}/${ver.interventions_total ?? '?'} interventions</span>`
      : `<span class="tag fail">not verified</span>`;
    const card = el(`<div class="card" style="margin-bottom:14px">
      <div class="spread">
        <div>
          <div style="font-size:15px;font-weight:600">${esc(p.title)}</div>
          <div class="small faint mono">${esc(p.id)} @ ${esc(p.version)} · ${esc(p.family)} · ${esc(p.authored_by)}</div>
        </div>
        <div class="row" style="gap:8px">${badge}
          <span class="chip">build ${money4(p.build_cost)}</span>
          <span class="chip">${p.predicates.length} predicates</span></div>
      </div>
      <div style="margin:12px 0 4px" class="mono small">
        <span class="faint">choose</span> ${esc(p.combine)}
        <span class="faint">→</span> <span class="ok">${esc(p.labels.yes)}</span> /
        <span class="bad">${esc(p.labels.no)}</span> /
        <span class="warn">${esc(p.labels.unknown)}</span> /
        <span style="color:var(--esc)">${esc(p.labels.escalate)}</span>
      </div>
      <div class="small faint" style="margin-bottom:10px">criteria: ${esc(p.criteria)}</div>
      <table><thead><tr><th>#</th><th>Judgment</th><th>Kind</th><th>May read</th><th>If missing</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>${p.predicates.map(x => `<tr>
        <td class="mono faint">${esc(x.id)}</td>
        <td>${esc(x.ask)}<div class="small faint">${esc(x.note||'')}</div></td>
        <td><span class="tag ${x.kind==='exact'?'code':'judge'}">${esc(x.kind)}</span></td>
        <td class="mono small">${esc((x.requires||[]).join(' ') || '—')}</td>
        <td class="mono small">${esc(x.on_missing)}${x.applies_if?`<div class="faint">only if ${esc(x.applies_if)}</div>`:''}</td>
      </tr>`).join('')}</tbody></table>
      ${entry.versions.length>1 ? `<div class="small dim" style="margin-top:10px">versions: ${
        entry.versions.map(v=>`<span class="chip ${v.version===entry.active?'yes':''}">${esc(v.version)}${v.verified?' ✓':''}</span>`).join(' ')}
        <span class="faint">· total build ${money4(entry.build_cost_total)}</span></div>` : ''}
    </div>`);
    host.appendChild(card);
  });

  // proposals
  const props = lib.proposals || [];
  const pc = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  pc.innerHTML = `<h3>Proposals from the second role</h3>
    <div class="small dim" style="margin-bottom:10px">Between runs, the frontier model is shown the
    cases where the small path was wrong and asked what is missing — a predicate, an exception, a source
    requirement, a different decomposition. A proposal does not change the library. It creates a
    candidate, and the candidate has to pass the intervention suite and the labelled set before it is
    admitted.</div>`;
  if (!props.length) {
    pc.innerHTML += `<div class="empty">No open proposals. ${
      (lib.library.find(l=>l.program.id==='PROC-01')||{program:{}}).program.version === '2.0'
      ? 'PR-1 was admitted as PROC-01 v2.0 — see the Interventions tab for what changed.' : ''}</div>`;
  }
  props.forEach(pr => {
    pc.appendChild(el(`<div style="border:1px solid var(--line);border-radius:8px;padding:12px;margin-bottom:10px">
      <div class="spread"><div style="font-weight:600">${esc(pr.title)}</div>
        <span class="chip">${esc(pr.kind)}</span></div>
      <div class="small dim" style="margin:8px 0">${esc(pr.motivation)}</div>
      <div class="mono small faint">${esc(pr.target)} → ${esc(pr.proposed_version)}
        · adds ${esc((pr.patch.add_predicates||[]).join(', '))}</div>
      <div class="mono small faint" style="margin-top:4px">${esc(pr.patch.combine_before)}</div>
      <div class="mono small" style="margin-top:2px;color:var(--acc)">${esc(pr.patch.combine_after)}</div>
      <div class="row" style="margin-top:10px"><button class="act" id="admit-${esc(pr.id)}">Build, verify, admit</button>
        <span class="small faint">${esc(pr.proposed_by)}</span></div>
    </div>`));
    const btn = pc.querySelector('#admit-'+pr.id);
    btn.onclick = async () => {
      if (!LIVE) { toast('Static snapshot. Run <span class="mono">python3 -m jig.server</span> to replay the admission live.'); return; }
      btn.disabled = true; btn.textContent = 'verifying…';
      try {
        const r = await post('/api/admit', {proposal: pr.id});
        const v = r.verification || {};
        toast(`<b>${esc(r.status)}</b> — ${v.interventions_passed}/${v.interventions_total} interventions, `+
              `${v.regression_passed}/${v.regression_total} labelled. Now active: PROC-01 v${esc(r.active||'')}.`,
              r.status === 'admitted' ? 'ok' : 'bad');
        setTimeout(()=>VIEWS.programs(), 1200);
      } catch(e){ toast(esc(e.message), 'bad'); btn.disabled=false; btn.textContent='Build, verify, admit'; }
    };
  });
  host.appendChild(pc);

  // proof
  if (Object.keys(proof).length){
    const pf = el('<div class="card"></div>');
    pf.innerHTML = `<h3>Before and after</h3>
      <table><thead><tr><th>Version</th><th class="num">Interventions</th><th class="num">Labelled</th>
      <th>Failing</th><th>Verdict</th></tr></thead><tbody>${
      Object.entries(proof).map(([k,v]) => `<tr>
        <td class="mono">PROC-01 @ ${esc(k)}</td>
        <td class="num">${v.interventions_passed}/${v.interventions_total}</td>
        <td class="num">${v.regression_passed}/${v.regression_total}</td>
        <td class="mono small faint">${esc(v.failures.join(', ') || '—')}</td>
        <td>${v.verified ? '<span class="tag pass">admitted</span>' : '<span class="tag fail">rejected</span>'}</td>
      </tr>`).join('')}</tbody></table>
      <div class="small faint" style="margin-top:8px">The proposal fixed the case it was written for and
      did not regress the rest. That is the whole check: not that the reasoning sounds right, but that the
      new version survives the suite the old one failed.</div>`;
    host.appendChild(pf);
  }
};

VIEWS.goggles = async () => {
  let v = null;
  try { v = await get('/api/verify'); } catch(e){}
  const main = document.getElementById('main');
  main.innerHTML = `<h2>Interventions</h2>
    <p class="lede">Agreement with a frontier answer is the weakest evidence available: two pipelines can
    reach the same verdict for different reasons, one of them wrong. So each program version is tested by
    <b>changing the world under it</b>. Flip a decisive fact and the verdict must change. Reword
    irrelevantly and it must not. Delete an indispensable source and it must refuse to decide. Move the
    date across a rule change and it must follow the rule in force. Leave the library's scope and it must
    escalate.</p>
    <div id="iv"></div>`;
  const host = main.querySelector('#iv');
  if (!v) { host.innerHTML = '<div class="empty">no verification data</div>'; return; }
  v.programs.forEach(pr => {
    const pass = pr.interventions.filter(i=>i.pass).length;
    const card = el(`<div class="card" style="margin-bottom:14px">
      <div class="spread">
        <div style="font-weight:600">${esc(pr.program)} @ ${esc(pr.version)}</div>
        <div class="row" style="gap:8px">
          <span class="tag ${pr.verified?'pass':'fail'}">${pr.verified?'verified':'rejected'}</span>
          <span class="chip">${pass}/${pr.interventions.length} interventions</span>
          <span class="chip">${pr.regression_passed}/${pr.regression_total} labelled</span>
        </div>
      </div>
      <table style="margin-top:10px"><thead><tr><th>Intervention</th><th>Kind</th><th>Expected</th>
      <th>Got</th><th>Why this is the test</th></tr></thead><tbody>${
        pr.interventions.map(i => `<tr>
          <td>${esc(i.title)}<div class="small faint mono">${esc(i.id)}</div></td>
          <td class="mono small faint">${esc(i.kind)}</td>
          <td><span class="chip">${esc(i.expect)}</span></td>
          <td><span class="chip ${i.pass?'yes':'no'}">${esc(i.got)}</span>
            ${i.pass?'':'<div class="small bad">'+esc(i.detail)+'</div>'}</td>
          <td class="small dim">${esc(i.rationale)}</td></tr>`).join('')}</tbody></table>
      ${pr.failures.length ? `<div class="small bad" style="margin-top:8px">failing: ${esc(pr.failures.join(', '))}</div>`:''}
    </div>`);
    host.appendChild(card);
  });
  const ba = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  ba.innerHTML = `<h3>What the interventions caught</h3>
    <div class="small dim">PROC-01 v1.0 passes three of five. It approves the Nimbus purchase because
    nothing in its structure asks about security reviews, and when the review register is deleted it keeps
    approving — <b>a program with no predicate for a source cannot miss that source</b>. That is the
    failure mode agreement-based evaluation cannot see, and it is why v1.0 was never admitted.</div>
    <div class="small dim" style="margin-top:8px">v2.0 adds two predicates and an applicability condition.
    It now defers when the register is gone and still approves when only the wording changed.</div>`;
  host.appendChild(ba);
};

VIEWS.arms = async () => {
  let b = null;
  try { b = await get('/api/bench'); } catch(e){}
  const main = document.getElementById('main');
  main.innerHTML = `<h2>Four pipelines, one axis</h2>
    <p class="lede">Same 15 labelled questions, same frozen corpus, same output contract. The frontier
    answer is never visible to the small pipelines. Labels are compared on a shared axis
    (YES / NO / DEFER / ESCALATE) because each program names its own outcomes. A wrong binary call where
    the honest answer was <i>I cannot tell</i> is counted as <b>overreach</b>, separately from a plain
    error.</p>
    <div id="b"></div>`;
  const host = main.querySelector('#b');
  if (!b) { host.innerHTML = '<div class="empty">no benchmark data</div>'; return; }

  const arms = ['A','B1','B2','B3'];
  const band = el(`<div class="banner">The frontier arm is <b>modelled</b>: its answers are the labelled
    ground truth, standing in for a real frontier run, and its cost and latency come from declared token
    rates. Every other number here is produced by executing the pipeline.</div>`);
  host.appendChild(band);

  const card = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  card.innerHTML = `<h3>Scoreboard</h3>
    <table><thead><tr><th>Arm</th><th class="num">Correct</th><th class="num">Material errors</th>
      <th class="num">Overreach</th><th class="num">Defer</th><th class="num">Escalate</th>
      <th class="num">Citation support</th><th class="num">Cost / decision</th><th class="num">Latency</th>
      <th class="num">Predicates</th></tr></thead><tbody>${
      arms.map(k => { const a = b.arms[k], s = a.score; return `<tr>
        <td><b>${k}</b> <span class="dim">${esc(a.title)}</span>
          ${k==='A'?'<span class="tag model">modelled</span>':''}</td>
        <td class="num">${s.correct}/${s.n}</td>
        <td class="num ${s.material_errors?'bad':''}">${s.material_errors}</td>
        <td class="num ${s.overreach?'bad':''}">${s.overreach}</td>
        <td class="num">${s.deferred}</td><td class="num">${s.escalated}</td>
        <td class="num">${(s.citation_support*100).toFixed(0)}%</td>
        <td class="num">${money(s.avg_cost_answered)}</td>
        <td class="num">${ms(s.avg_latency_answered_ms)}</td>
        <td class="num">${s.avg_predicates}</td></tr>`;}).join('')}</tbody></table>
    <div class="small faint" style="margin-top:8px">Cost per decision excludes the one escalated question,
    which by design spends a frontier call; escalations are counted in their own column.</div>`;
  host.appendChild(card);

  // per-question matrix
  const mx = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  const ids = b.arms.B3.rows.map(r => r.id);
  const cell = (k, id) => {
    const r = b.arms[k].rows.find(x => x.id === id);
    if (!r) return '<td></td>';
    const ok = r.canon === r.expect_canon;
    const cls = r.canon === 'DEFER' ? 'unk' : r.canon === 'ESCALATE' ? 'esc' : (r.canon === 'YES' ? 'yes' : 'no');
    return `<td class="nowrap"><span class="chip ${cls}">${esc(r.label)}</span>${ok?'':' <span class="bad small">✗</span>'}</td>`;
  };
  mx.innerHTML = `<h3>Every question</h3>
    <table><thead><tr><th>#</th><th>Program</th><th>Expected</th>${arms.map(k=>`<th>${k}</th>`).join('')}
      <th>Why</th></tr></thead><tbody>${
      b.arms.B3.rows.map(r => `<tr>
        <td class="mono faint">${esc(r.id)}</td><td class="mono small">${esc(r.program||'—')}</td>
        <td><span class="chip">${esc(r.expect)}</span></td>
        ${arms.map(k=>cell(k,r.id)).join('')}
        <td class="small dim">${esc(clamp(r.why, 110))}</td></tr>`).join('')}</tbody></table>`;
  host.appendChild(mx);

  // localisation
  const loc = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  const bad = (b.localisation||[]).filter(x => x.status !== 'correct');
  loc.innerHTML = `<h3>Where the single pass breaks</h3>
    <div class="small dim" style="margin-bottom:8px">Each failure is localised rather than totalled:
    rerun with the program pinned (was the decomposition missing?), then with the decisive document handed
    over (was retrieval missing it?). What survives both is a judgment problem.</div>` +
    (bad.length ? `<table><thead><tr><th>#</th><th>Component</th><th>What the injection showed</th></tr></thead><tbody>${
      bad.map(x => `<tr><td class="mono faint">${esc(x.id)}</td>
        <td><span class="chip ${x.status==='scope'?'esc':x.status==='decomposition'?'unk':'no'}">${esc(x.status)}</span></td>
        <td class="small dim">${esc(x.detail)}</td></tr>`).join('')}</tbody></table>`
      : '<div class="empty">no failures to localise</div>');
  host.appendChild(loc);

  // economics
  const e = b.economics;
  const ec = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  ec.innerHTML = `<h3>Economics</h3>
    <div class="cols-2">
      <div><div class="kv">
        <span>one-time build (all programs)</span><span>${money4(e.one_time_build_cost)}</span>
        <span>programs / versions</span><span>${e.programs} / ${e.versions}</span>
        <span>frontier per question</span><span>${money(e.frontier_per_question,4)}</span>
        <span>reused per decision</span><span>${money(e.reused_per_question)}</span>
        <span>ratio</span><span class="ok">${e.ratio}×</span>
        <span>break-even</span><span>${e.break_even_questions} questions</span>
        <span>build cost as frontier questions</span><span>${e.build_questions_equivalent}</span>
      </div></div>
      <div><div class="kv">
        <span>latency, frontier</span><span>${ms(e.latency.frontier_ms)}</span>
        <span>latency, reused</span><span>${ms(e.latency.reused_ms_answered)}</span>
        <span>ratio</span><span class="ok">${e.latency.ratio}×</span>
        <span>escalation rate</span><span>${(e.escalation_rate*100).toFixed(1)}%</span>
        <span>judge calls / question</span><span>${e.judge_calls_per_question}</span>
        <span>frontier calls / question</span><span>${e.frontier_calls_saved_per_question} (escalations only)</span>
      </div>
      <div class="small faint" style="margin-top:10px">Escalations are counted as frontier calls and
      included in the latency and cost columns of their own row.</div></div>
    </div>`;
  host.appendChild(ec);
};

VIEWS.reuse = async () => {
  let r = null;
  try { r = await get('/api/reuse'); } catch(e){}
  const main = document.getElementById('main');
  main.innerHTML = `<h2>Pay once, refresh the facts</h2>
    <p class="lede">The structure is the reusable object. The facts are not: they live in the corpus and
    the predicates re-read them. So a vendor being suspended, a budget being topped up, a threshold
    moving or a memo being withdrawn changes the verdict <b>without anyone rebuilding a program</b>.
    Held-out questions are the ones the programs were never authored against.</p>
    <div id="r"></div>`;
  const host = main.querySelector('#r');
  if (!r) { host.innerHTML = '<div class="empty">no reuse data</div>'; return; }

  const top = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  top.innerHTML = `<h3>Held-out questions</h3>
    <div class="kv" style="max-width:520px">
      <span>build set (authored against)</span><span>${esc(r.build_set.join(', '))}</span>
      <span>held-out</span><span>${esc(r.held_out.join(', '))}</span>
      <span>cost per answered held-out question</span><span>${money(r.held_out_cost_per_answered)}</span>
      <span>frontier per question</span><span>${money(r.frontier_per_question,4)}</span>
      <span>ratio on unseen questions</span><span class="ok">${r.held_out_ratio}×</span>
      <span>held-out escalations</span><span>${r.held_out_escalations} (${money4(r.held_out_escalation_cost)})</span>
      <span>rebuilds required</span><span>${r.rebuilds_required}</span>
    </div>`;
  host.appendChild(top);

  // savings curve
  const sc = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  const max = Math.max(...r.savings.map(s=>s.frontier));
  sc.innerHTML = `<h3>Cumulative cost</h3>
    <table><thead><tr><th class="num">Questions</th><th class="num">Frontier</th>
      <th class="num">Reused (incl. build)</th><th class="num">Marginal only</th><th class="num">Ratio</th>
      <th style="width:34%"></th></tr></thead><tbody>${
      r.savings.map(s => `<tr>
        <td class="num">${s.n}</td><td class="num">${money4(s.frontier)}</td>
        <td class="num">${money4(s.reused)}</td><td class="num faint">${money4(s.marginal)}</td>
        <td class="num ${s.ratio>1?'ok':'warn'}">${s.ratio}×</td>
        <td><div class="meter"><i style="width:${Math.min(100, 100*s.frontier/max)}%"></i></div></td>
      </tr>`).join('')}</tbody></table>
    <div class="small faint" style="margin-top:8px">Bars show frontier cost at the same question count.
    Below roughly ${r.savings.find(s=>s.ratio>1)?.n || 5} questions the library has not paid for itself.</div>`;
  host.appendChild(sc);

  // variants
  const vc = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  vc.innerHTML = `<h3>Fresh facts, same programs</h3>
    <div class="small dim" style="margin-bottom:10px">Each variant edits the corpus or the request date and
    re-runs every question through the same program version. Nothing is rebuilt; ${
      r.variants_passed}/${r.variants_total} variants behave as required.</div>` +
    r.variants.map(v => `<div style="border:1px solid var(--line);border-radius:8px;padding:12px;margin-bottom:10px">
      <div class="spread"><div style="font-weight:600">${esc(v.title)}</div>
        <span class="tag ${v.pass?'pass':'fail'}">${v.checks_passed}/${v.checks_total} checks</span></div>
      <div class="small dim" style="margin:6px 0">${esc(v.note)}</div>
      <div class="small mono">${(v.changed||[]).length
        ? v.changed.map(c=>`${esc(c.id)}: <span class="faint">${esc(c.before)}</span> → <span class="ok">${esc(c.after)}</span>`).join(' &nbsp;·&nbsp; ')
        : '<span class="faint">no verdict moved</span>'}</div>
      <div class="small faint" style="margin-top:6px">rebuild cost ${money4(v.build_cost)} ·
        per-run cost ${money(v.per_run_cost)} (unchanged)</div>
    </div>`).join('');
  host.appendChild(vc);
};

VIEWS.method = async () => {
  let s = null;
  try { s = await get('/api/state'); } catch(e){}
  const main = document.getElementById('main');
  main.innerHTML = `<h2>What is measured, and what is modelled</h2>
    <p class="lede">The pipeline is executed: every verdict on this page came from running the code.
    Some of the surrounding numbers are not executed, and they are labelled everywhere they appear.</p>
    <div id="m"></div>`;
  const host = main.querySelector('#m');
  if (!s) { host.innerHTML = '<div class="empty">no state</div>'; return; }
  const L = s.labels;
  const rows = [['Cost model', L.cost_model], ['Frontier arm', L.frontier_arm],
                ['Corpus', L.corpus], ['Judge', L.judge], ['Error budget', L.error_budget]];
  const c1 = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  c1.innerHTML = `<h3>Labels</h3><table><tbody>${
    rows.map(([k,v]) => `<tr><td style="width:130px" class="mono small faint">${esc(k)}</td><td class="small">${esc(v)}</td></tr>`).join('')
  }</tbody></table>`;
  host.appendChild(c1);

  const c2 = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  c2.innerHTML = `<h3>Rates in force</h3>
    <table><thead><tr><th>Call</th><th class="num">$/Mtok in</th><th class="num">$/Mtok out</th>
      <th class="num">tokens in</th><th class="num">tokens out</th><th class="num">seconds</th>
      <th class="num">$/call</th></tr></thead><tbody>${
      Object.entries(s.rates).map(([k,r]) => {
        const cost = (r.tokens_in*r.in_rate + r.tokens_out*r.out_rate)/1e6;
        return `<tr><td class="mono">${esc(k)}</td><td class="num">${r.in_rate.toFixed(2)}</td>
          <td class="num">${r.out_rate.toFixed(2)}</td><td class="num">${r.tokens_in.toLocaleString()}</td>
          <td class="num">${r.tokens_out.toLocaleString()}</td><td class="num">${r.seconds}</td>
          <td class="num">${money(cost)}</td></tr>`;}).join('')}</tbody></table>
      <div class="small faint" style="margin-top:8px">Judge calls carry evidence: each extra excerpt adds
      ${s.rates.judge.tok_per_doc} input tokens and ${s.rates.judge.sec_per_doc}s, which is why scoping
      retrieval per predicate shows up in the cost column at all.</div>`;
  host.appendChild(c2);

  const c3 = el('<div class="card"></div>');
  c3.innerHTML = `<h3>Known limitations</h3>
    <div class="small dim">
      <p style="margin:0 0 8px"><b>Registers are read as current state.</b> Versioned policy documents
      are resolved by the date in force, but a register or memo is a point-in-time record; its
      last-updated date is shown with the citation rather than used to suppress it for earlier questions.
      A real deployment would need snapshotted registers.</p>
      <p style="margin:0 0 8px"><b>The judge is offline here.</b> Judge predicates run on entailment
      patterns, not a model. The program structure is what is being tested, and it does not change when a
      model is plugged in — <span class="mono">LLMJudge</span> sends exactly the retrieved excerpts and
      parses a structured answer.</p>
      <p style="margin:0 0 8px"><b>Fifteen questions is a test fixture, not a benchmark.</b> The labelled
      set is small enough that every ground truth can be read off the corpus by hand, which is the point
      at this stage, and far too small to estimate an error rate.</p>
      <p style="margin:0"><b>The frontier arm is a stand-in.</b> Treat its column as the price of the
      alternative, not as a measurement of any particular model.</p>
    </div>`;
  host.appendChild(c3);
};

/* ---------- shell ---------- */

     max-width:420px;z-index:9;box-shadow:0 8px 30px rgba(0,0,0,.6)">${html}</div>`);
  document.body.appendChild(t);
  setTimeout(()=>t.remove(), 6000);
}

FILE: bake_preview.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Bake a live run into web/app.html so the file previews with real data offline.

The preview iframe has no network, so the page falls back to a snapshot. The
snapshot is produced by starting the real server, admitting the proposal through
the real HTTP API, and reading the real endpoints — not by hand-writing example
JSON. Anything baked here therefore matches exactly what a live run would show,
including the economics of the reactive intervention suite.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.request

ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
PORT = int(os.environ.get("JIG_BAKE_PORT", "8011"))
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
APP = os.path.join(ROOT, "web", "app.html")

PRESETS = [
    "Can we approve a $4,200 analytics subscription from Acme Analytics on 2026-11-05, charged to ENG-TOOLS?",
    "Can we approve a $4,200 hosting purchase from Nimbus Hosting on 2026-11-05, charged to ENG-TOOLS?",
    "Can we approve $3,800 of conference travel for J. Reyes to attend DataOps Summit 2026 on 2026-11-05, charged to MKT-EVENTS?",
    "Can we approve a $4,200 analytics subscription from Acme Analytics on 2026-05-01, charged to ENG-TOOLS?",
    "Can we approve a $900 support plan from Halcyon Data on 2026-11-05, charged to ENG-TOOLS?",
    "Should we renew the office lease for our Boston office in 2027?",
    "Can we onboard Kestrel Hardware for hardware supply on 2026-11-05?",
    "Can we proceed without prior approval on an emergency purchase of $9,000 of ventilation safety equipment on 2026-11-05, CFO notified within 24 hours: yes?",
]


def get(path: str):
    with urllib.request.urlopen(BASE + path, timeout=120) as r:
        return json.loads(r.read())


def post(path: str, body: dict):
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        BASE + path, data=json.dumps(body).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=180) as r:
        return json.loads(r.read())


def wait_up(deadline=30.0):
    t0 = time.time()
    while time.time() - t0 < deadline:
        try:
            get("/api/health")
            return True
        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001
            time.sleep(0.3)
    return False


def main() -> int:
    env = dict(os.environ, PYTHONPATH=ROOT)
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-m", "jig.server", str(PORT)],
                            cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,
                            stderr=subprocess.PIPE)
    try:
        if not wait_up():
            print("server did not start", file=sys.stderr)
            print(proc.stderr.read().decode()[:2000], file=sys.stderr)
            return 1

        # admit the proposal through the real API so the snapshot shows the
        # library in its adjudicated state
        admit = post("/api/admit", {"proposal": "PR-1"})
        print(f"admission: {admit['status']} -> PROC-01 v{admit.get('active')}")

        snap = {}
        for ep in ("/api/state", "/api/library", "/api/verify", "/api/bench",
                   "/api/reuse", "/api/interventions"):
            snap[ep] = get(ep)
            print(f"  baked {ep}")

        for q in PRESETS:
            res = post("/api/decide", {"question": q})
            snap["/api/decide:" + q] = res
            print(f"  baked decision: {res['decision']['label']:<20} {q[:56]}…")

        payload = json.dumps(snap, separators=(",", ":"))
        html = open(APP, encoding="utf-8").read()
        marker = "/*__SNAPSHOT__*/ null"
        if marker not in html:
            print("snapshot marker missing from app.html", file=sys.stderr)
            return 1
        html = html.replace(marker, payload)
        open(APP, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(html)
        print(f"\nwrote {len(html):,} bytes into web/app.html "
              f"(snapshot {len(payload):,} bytes, {len(snap)} endpoints)")
        return 0
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        try:
            proc.wait(timeout=5)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            proc.kill()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

FILE: bench.py
================================================
"""
jig.bench — the four arms, scored on one axis.

  A   frontier, end to end       one open-ended call over the whole corpus. Its
                                 answers are the labelled ground truth and its
                                 cost/latency are declared rates: modelled, not
                                 measured. Say so wherever it is shown.
  B1  single answer + retrieval   one retrieval, one judgment, no decomposition
  B2  decomposition + retrieval   every predicate resolved, nothing skipped
  B3  reused program + targeted   the library's program, gap-directed retrieval,
                                 early stop

Programs name their outcomes for their own domain (CLEARED, PROCEED, BLOCKED), so
everything is compared on a shared YES / NO / DEFER / ESCALATE axis and shown in
the program's own words.

Failures are localised rather than totalled: when the small path is wrong, the
same question is rerun with the program pinned (was the decomposition missing?),
then with the decisive document handed over (was retrieval missing it?). What
survives both is a predicate-evaluation problem.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

from .core import call_cost, call_seconds, frontier_cost
from .corpus import Corpus
from .engine import Engine, extract_slots
from .programs import LABELLED

ARMS = [
    ("A", "frontier, end to end"),
    ("B1", "single answer + retrieval"),
    ("B2", "predicate decomposition + retrieval"),
    ("B3", "reused program + targeted retrieval"),
]

WITHDRAWN = {"DEFER", "ESCALATE"}


def expect_canon(engine: Engine, q: dict) -> str:
    if not q["program"]:
        return q["expect"]
    prog = engine.library.get(q["program"])
    return prog.canon(q["expect"])


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# arm B1: one retrieval, one judgment
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def arm_single(engine: Engine, question: str, corpus: Corpus | None = None,
               forced: list[str] | None = None) -> dict:
    """The honest shape of "attach the sources and ask".

    One retrieval for the whole question, then apply the checks the retrieved
    documents look like they support. It has no notion of which predicate is
    still unsettled, so it cannot look for exceptions, cannot notice that the
    governing rule changed, and cannot decline to answer.
    """
    corpus = corpus or engine.corpus
    slots = extract_slots(question, corpus)
    as_of = slots.get("as_of", "2026-11-05")
    excerpts = corpus.search(question, as_of, only=None, k=8)
    if forced:
        for doc_id in forced:
            doc = corpus.get(doc_id)
            if doc and all(e.doc_id != doc_id for e in excerpts):
                from .core import Excerpt
                excerpts.append(Excerpt(doc.id, doc.title, doc.date, doc.text,
                                        doc.text[:200], 0.0, doc.effective))
    notes, cites = [], []
    blocked = False
    amount, vendor, budget = (slots.get("amount"), slots.get("vendor"),
                              slots.get("budget_line"))

    pol = next((e for e in sorted(excerpts, key=lambda e: e.date, reverse=True)
                if e.doc_id.startswith("policy/procurement")), None)
    if pol and amount:
        m = re.search(r"up to \$([\d,]+) may be approved", pol.text)
        if m and amount > float(m.group(1).replace(",", "")):
            blocked = True
            notes.append(f"${amount:,.0f} is above the manager limit in {pol.doc_id}")
            cites.append(pol.doc_id)

    if vendor:
        reg = next((e for e in excerpts if e.doc_id == "registry/vendors"), None)
        if reg and re.search(rf"{re.escape(vendor)}[^\n]*suspended", reg.text, re.I):
            blocked = True
            notes.append(f"{vendor} is suspended")
            cites.append(reg.doc_id)
        sec = next((e for e in excerpts if e.doc_id == "registry/security"), None)
        if sec and re.search(rf"{re.escape(vendor)}[^\n]*expired", sec.text, re.I):
            blocked = True
            notes.append(f"{vendor}'s security review is expired")
            cites.append(sec.doc_id)

    if budget:
        breg = next((e for e in excerpts if e.doc_id == "registry/budget"), None)
        if breg:
            line = next((l for l in breg.text.splitlines() if budget in l), "")
            if line and ("frozen" in line.lower() or "closed" in line.lower()):
                blocked = True
                notes.append(f"{budget} is frozen or closed")
                cites.append(breg.doc_id)

    return {"arm": "B1", "question": question,
            "label": "DENY" if blocked else "APPROVE",
            "canon": "NO" if blocked else "YES",
            "cites": cites,
            "notes": notes or ["no blocking signal in the top 8 documents"],
            "evaluated": 1, "judge_calls": 1,
            "cost": round(call_cost("planner") + call_cost("judge"), 6),
            "latency_ms": (call_seconds("planner") + call_seconds("judge")) * 1000,
            "gaps": []}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# scoring
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def score_arm(rows: list[dict]) -> dict:
    n = len(rows)
    answered = [r for r in rows if r["canon"] not in WITHDRAWN]
    material = [r for r in answered
                if r["expect_canon"] not in WITHDRAWN
                and r["canon"] != r["expect_canon"]]
    overreach = [r for r in answered if r["expect_canon"] in WITHDRAWN]
    cited = sum(1 for r in rows if r.get("decisive") and
                r["decisive"] in (r.get("cites") or []))
    cite_base = sum(1 for r in rows if r.get("decisive"))
    ans = [r for r in rows if r["canon"] != "ESCALATE"]
    return {
        "n": n,
        "correct": sum(1 for r in rows if r["canon"] == r["expect_canon"]),
        "accuracy": round(sum(1 for r in rows if r["canon"] == r["expect_canon"]) / n, 3),
        "answered": len(answered),
        "coverage": round(len(answered) / n, 3),
        "material_errors": len(material),
        "material_error_rate_at_coverage": round(len(material) / max(1, len(answered)), 3),
        "overreach": len(overreach),
        "deferred": sum(1 for r in rows if r["canon"] == "DEFER"),
        "escalated": sum(1 for r in rows if r["canon"] == "ESCALATE"),
        "citation_support": round(cited / cite_base, 3) if cite_base else 0.0,
        "avg_cost": round(sum(r["cost"] for r in rows) / n, 6),
        "avg_cost_answered": round(sum(r["cost"] for r in ans) / max(1, len(ans)), 6),
        "avg_latency_ms": round(sum(r["latency_ms"] for r in rows) / n, 0),
        "avg_latency_answered_ms": round(sum(r["latency_ms"] for r in ans) / max(1, len(ans)), 0),
        "avg_predicates": round(sum(r.get("evaluated", 0) for r in rows) / n, 1),
        "judge_calls": sum(r.get("judge_calls", 0) for r in rows),
        "judge_calls_per_question": round(sum(r.get("judge_calls", 0) for r in rows) / n, 2),
    }


def run_arm(engine: Engine, arm: str, questions: list[dict],
            corpus: Corpus | None = None) -> dict:
    corpus = corpus or engine.corpus
    rows = []
    for q in questions:
        ec = expect_canon(engine, q)
        if arm == "A":
            row = {"arm": "A", "label": q["expect"], "canon": ec,
                   "verdict": "YES" if ec not in WITHDRAWN else ec,
                   "cites": [], "notes": ["frontier run (modelled): answers are the "
                                          "labelled ground truth"],
                   "evaluated": 0, "judge_calls": 1,
                   "cost": frontier_cost(),
                   "latency_ms": call_seconds("frontier") * 1000, "gaps": []}
        elif arm == "B1":
            row = arm_single(engine, q["question"], corpus)
        else:
            prog = engine.library.get(q["program"]) if q["program"] else None
            d = engine.run(q["question"], program=prog, corpus=corpus,
                           targeted=(arm == "B3"), scoped=(arm == "B3"))
            row = {"arm": arm, "label": d.label,
                   "canon": (prog.canon(d.label) if prog else
                             ("ESCALATE" if d.verdict == "ESCALATE" else "DEFER")),
                   "verdict": d.verdict, "cites": [c.doc_id for c in d.citations],
                   "notes": [f.rationale for f in d.findings][:3],
                   "evaluated": d.evaluated,
                   "judge_calls": d.cost.get("judge_calls", 0),
                   "cost": d.cost["total"], "latency_ms": d.latency_ms,
                   "gaps": d.gaps}
        row.update({"id": q["id"], "program": q["program"], "expect": q["expect"],
                    "expect_canon": ec, "decisive": q["decisive"], "why": q["why"],
                    "question": q["question"]})
        # the frontier's citations are not observable from a single open call, so
        # they are excluded from the citation measure rather than scored as zero
        if arm == "A":
            row["decisive"] = ""
        rows.append(row)
    return {"arm": arm, "rows": rows, "score": score_arm(rows)}


def run_all(engine: Engine, questions: list[dict] | None = None) -> dict:
    questions = questions or LABELLED
    arms = {}
    for arm, title in ARMS:
        arms[arm] = run_arm(engine, arm, questions)
        arms[arm]["title"] = title
    return arms


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# localisation
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def localise(engine: Engine, q: dict) -> dict:
    """When the small path is wrong: decomposition, retrieval, or judgment?"""
    base = arm_single(engine, q["question"])
    ec = expect_canon(engine, q)
    if base["canon"] == ec:
        return {"id": q["id"], "status": "correct", "question": q["question"],
                "detail": "single pass was right", "got": base["label"],
                "expect": q["expect"]}

    if not q["program"]:
        return {"id": q["id"], "status": "scope",
                "question": q["question"], "got": base["label"],
                "expect": q["expect"],
                "detail": "no program covers this question, so declining is the "
                          "correct answer. A single pass has no scope to consult "
                          "and answers anyway."}

    pinned = engine.run(q["question"], program_id=q["program"])
    if pinned.label == q["expect"]:
        return {"id": q["id"], "status": "decomposition",
                "question": q["question"], "got": base["label"],
                "expect": q["expect"],
                "detail": f"single pass answers {base['label']}; the same sources "
                          f"with the program's decomposition give {pinned.label}. "
                          "The missing part was the structure, not the evidence."}

    packet = arm_single(engine, q["question"], forced=[q["decisive"]])
    if packet["canon"] == ec:
        return {"id": q["id"], "status": "retrieval",
                "question": q["question"], "got": pinned.label,
                "expect": q["expect"],
                "detail": f"with the program's structure the answer was still "
                          f"{pinned.label}; handing over {q['decisive']} fixes it."}

    return {"id": q["id"], "status": "judgment",
            "question": q["question"], "got": pinned.label,
            "expect": q["expect"],
            "detail": f"program pinned and {q['decisive'] or 'the packet'} supplied, "
                      f"still {pinned.label}. The predicate evaluation is what "
                      "needs attention."}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# economics
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def economics(engine: Engine, arms: dict) -> dict:
    one_time = engine.library.build_totals()
    b3 = arms["B3"]["score"]["avg_cost_answered"]
    f = frontier_cost()
    saving = f - b3
    n = arms["B3"]["score"]["n"]
    return {
        "one_time_build_cost": one_time["one_time_cost"],
        "programs": one_time["programs"], "versions": one_time["versions"],
        "frontier_per_question": round(f, 4),
        "reused_per_question": round(b3, 6),
        "ratio": round(f / b3, 1) if b3 else None,
        "build_questions_equivalent": round(one_time["one_time_cost"] / f, 2),
        "break_even_questions": round(one_time["one_time_cost"] / saving, 2) if saving > 0 else None,
        "block": {
            "frontier_total": round(f * n, 3),
            "reused_total_amortised": round(b3 * n + one_time["one_time_cost"], 4),
            "reused_total_excluding_build": round(b3 * n, 4),
        },
        "latency": {
            "frontier_ms": 92000,
            "reused_ms_answered": arms["B3"]["score"]["avg_latency_answered_ms"],
            "reused_ms_blended": arms["B3"]["score"]["avg_latency_ms"],
            "ratio": round(92000 / max(1e-9, arms["B3"]["score"]["avg_latency_answered_ms"]), 1),
        },
        "escalations": arms["B3"]["score"]["escalated"],
        "escalation_rate": round(arms["B3"]["score"]["escalated"] / n, 3),
        "judge_calls_per_question": arms["B3"]["score"]["judge_calls_per_question"],
        "frontier_calls_saved_per_question": 1.0,
    }



================================================
FILE: core.py
================================================
"""
jig.core — the objects a decision is made of.

A decision program is a reusable structure: typed predicates, the knowledge each
one needs, what to do when that knowledge is absent, how the answers combine, and
where to stop. It is authored once for a family of questions and then rebound to
fresh facts.

Nothing here knows about models. The planner, judges and retrievers are plugged
in behind interfaces so the same program can run against a reference judge
offline or against a real model endpoint.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# three-valued logic
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

TRUE, FALSE, UNKNOWN = "yes", "no", "unknown"
VALUES = (TRUE, FALSE, UNKNOWN)


def kleene_not(v: str) -> str:
    return {TRUE: FALSE, FALSE: TRUE, UNKNOWN: UNKNOWN}[v]


def kleene_and(vals: list[str]) -> str:
    if any(v == FALSE for v in vals):
        return FALSE
    if any(v == UNKNOWN for v in vals):
        return UNKNOWN
    return TRUE


def kleene_or(vals: list[str]) -> str:
    if any(v == TRUE for v in vals):
        return TRUE
    if any(v == UNKNOWN for v in vals):
        return UNKNOWN
    return FALSE


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# expression tree over predicate ids
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Node:
    op: str                      # "and" | "or" | "not" | "pred" | "const"
    args: tuple = ()

    def preds(self) -> set[str]:
        if self.op == "pred":
            return {self.args[0]}
        if self.op == "const":
            return set()
        out: set[str] = set()
        for a in self.args:
            out |= a.preds()
        return out

    def evaluate(self, values: dict[str, str]) -> str:
        if self.op == "const":
            return self.args[0]
        if self.op == "pred":
            return values.get(self.args[0], UNKNOWN)
        if self.op == "not":
            return kleene_not(self.args[0].evaluate(values))
        if self.op == "and":
            return kleene_and([a.evaluate(values) for a in self.args])
        if self.op == "or":
            return kleene_or([a.evaluate(values) for a in self.args])
        raise ValueError(self.op)

    PREC = {"or": 1, "and": 2, "not": 3, "pred": 4, "const": 4}

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        """Readable form: parentheses only where precedence needs them."""
        if self.op == "const":
            return str(self.args[0])
        if self.op == "pred":
            return self.args[0]
        if self.op == "not":
            inner = self.args[0]
            text = str(inner)
            return f"NOT {text}" if inner.op in ("pred", "not", "const") else f"NOT ({text})"
        joiner = " AND " if self.op == "and" else " OR "
        mine = self.PREC[self.op]
        parts = []
        for a in self.args:
            t = str(a)
            parts.append(t if self.PREC[a.op] >= mine else f"({t})")
        return joiner.join(parts)


def P(name: str) -> Node:
    return Node("pred", (name,))


def AND(*a: Node) -> Node:
    return Node("and", tuple(a))


def OR(*a: Node) -> Node:
    return Node("or", tuple(a))


def NOT(a: Node) -> Node:
    return Node("not", (a,))


def parse_expr(text: str) -> Node:
    """Tiny parser for programs authored as strings: P1 AND NOT P3, parens, OR."""
    toks = text.replace("(", " ( ").replace(")", " ) ").split()

    def parse_or(i):
        node, i = parse_and(i)
        while i < len(toks) and toks[i].upper() == "OR":
            rhs, i = parse_and(i + 1)
            node = OR(node, rhs)
        return node, i

    def parse_and(i):
        node, i = parse_unary(i)
        while i < len(toks) and toks[i].upper() == "AND":
            rhs, i = parse_unary(i + 1)
            node = AND(node, rhs)
        return node, i

    def parse_unary(i):
        t = toks[i]
        if t.upper() == "NOT":
            node, i = parse_unary(i + 1)
            return NOT(node), i
        if t == "(":
            node, i = parse_or(i + 1)
            assert toks[i] == ")", toks[i]
            return node, i + 1
        return P(t), i + 1

    node, i = parse_or(0)
    if i != len(toks):
        raise ValueError(f"trailing tokens in expression: {toks[i:]}")
    return node


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# predicates
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class Predicate:
    """One bounded judgment the decision depends on.

    `check` names a deterministic operator (exact predicates) or "judge" for a
    predicate a model must evaluate over supplied evidence. `requires` is the
    knowledge this predicate is allowed to read; `on_missing` is what happens
    when that knowledge cannot be found.
    """
    id: str
    ask: str
    kind: str                     # "exact" | "judge" | "exception"
    check: str                    # operator name, or "judge"
    params: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    requires: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    on_missing: str = UNKNOWN     # no | unknown | escalate
    stops_if: str | None = None   # e.g. "no" -> stop this run and decide
    discriminative: bool = True   # can it change the outcome on its own?
    applies_if: str | None = None  # slot condition; else the predicate is n/a
    note: str = ""

    def public(self) -> dict:
        return {"id": self.id, "ask": self.ask, "kind": self.kind,
                "check": self.check, "params": self.params,
                "requires": self.requires, "on_missing": self.on_missing,
                "stops_if": self.stops_if, "discriminative": self.discriminative,
                "note": self.note}


@dataclass
class Program:
    """A reusable way to decide, with the tests that justify reusing it."""
    id: str
    family: str
    title: str
    version: str
    question_template: str
    slots: dict[str, str]                  # slot -> human description
    criteria: str                           # decision criteria (owner-supplied)
    as_of: str                              # how the effective date is chosen
    predicates: list[Predicate]
    combine: str                            # expression over predicate ids
    yes_label: str = "APPROVE"
    no_label: str = "DENY"
    unknown_label: str = "DEFER"
    escalate_label: str = "ESCALATE"
    route_keywords: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    route_requires: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    authored_by: str = "frontier (authoring role)"
    build_cost: float = 0.0                 # one-time, USD
    build_tokens_in: int = 0
    build_tokens_out: int = 0
    tests: list[dict] = field(default_factory=list)
    verified: bool = False
    verification: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    proposals: list[dict] = field(default_factory=list)
    lineage: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)

    def canon(self, label: str) -> str:
        """Map this program's vocabulary onto the shared YES/NO/WITHDRAW axis.

        Programs name their outcomes for their own domain (CLEARED, PROCEED,
        BLOCKED). Scoring across arms needs one axis, so every label is reported
        with its canonical form rather than being compared literally.
        """
        return {self.yes_label: "YES", self.no_label: "NO",
                self.unknown_label: "DEFER",
                self.escalate_label: "ESCALATE"}.get(label, "?")

    def expr(self) -> Node:
        return parse_expr(self.combine)

    def predicate(self, pid: str) -> Predicate:
        for p in self.predicates:
            if p.id == pid:
                return p
        raise KeyError(pid)

    def public(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "id": self.id, "family": self.family, "title": self.title,
            "version": self.version,
            "question_template": self.question_template,
            "slots": self.slots, "criteria": self.criteria, "as_of": self.as_of,
            "predicates": [p.public() for p in self.predicates],
            "combine": self.combine,
            "combine_pretty": str(self.expr()),
            "labels": {"yes": self.yes_label, "no": self.no_label,
                       "unknown": self.unknown_label,
                       "escalate": self.escalate_label},
            "route_keywords": self.route_keywords,
            "route_requires": self.route_requires,
            "authored_by": self.authored_by,
            "build_cost": round(self.build_cost, 4),
            "build_tokens": {"in": self.build_tokens_in, "out": self.build_tokens_out},
            "tests": self.tests,
            "verified": self.verified,
            "verification": self.verification,
            "proposals": self.proposals,
            "lineage": self.lineage,
        }

    def clone(self) -> "Program":
        import copy
        return copy.deepcopy(self)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# evidence, findings, decisions
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class Excerpt:
    doc_id: str
    title: str
    date: str
    text: str
    quote: str
    score: float = 0.0
    effective: str | None = None
    superseded: bool = False

    def public(self) -> dict:
        return {"doc_id": self.doc_id, "title": self.title, "date": self.date,
                "quote": self.quote, "score": round(self.score, 3),
                "effective": self.effective, "superseded": self.superseded}


@dataclass
class Finding:
    """What one predicate resolved to, and on what evidence."""
    predicate: str
    answer: str
    confidence: float
    citations: list[Excerpt]
    rationale: str
    evaluator: str            # "code" | "judge" | "skipped"
    ms: float = 0.0
    gap: str | None = None
    cost: float = 0.0

    def public(self) -> dict:
        return {"predicate": self.predicate, "answer": self.answer,
                "confidence": round(self.confidence, 3),
                "citations": [c.public() for c in self.citations],
                "rationale": self.rationale, "evaluator": self.evaluator,
                "ms": round(self.ms, 2), "gap": self.gap,
                "cost": round(self.cost, 5)}


@dataclass
class Decision:
    question: str
    program_id: str
    program_version: str
    slots: dict[str, str]
    as_of: str
    verdict: str
    label: str
    findings: list[Finding]
    gaps: list[str]
    citations: list[Excerpt]
    stopped_early: bool
    skipped: list[dict]
    trace: list[dict]
    cost: dict
    latency_ms: float
    evaluated: int = 0

    def public(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "question": self.question, "program_id": self.program_id,
            "program_version": self.program_version, "slots": self.slots,
            "as_of": self.as_of, "verdict": self.verdict, "label": self.label,
            "findings": [f.public() for f in self.findings],
            "gaps": self.gaps,
            "citations": [c.public() for c in self.citations],
            "stopped_early": self.stopped_early, "skipped": self.skipped,
            "trace": self.trace, "cost": self.cost,
            "latency_ms": round(self.latency_ms, 1),
            "evaluated": self.evaluated,
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# cost model
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Declared rates, not measured ones: what a run WOULD cost at these prices. The
# call counts feeding them are real, so the comparison between pipelines is
# internally consistent and the arithmetic is auditable.

RATES = {
    #                 in $/Mtok  out $/Mtok  typical in  out   seconds
    "frontier": dict(in_rate=3.00, out_rate=15.00, tokens_in=42_000, tokens_out=3_500, seconds=92.0),
    "planner": dict(in_rate=0.10, out_rate=0.40, tokens_in=1_400, tokens_out=320, seconds=2.1),
    "judge": dict(in_rate=0.10, out_rate=0.40, tokens_in=950, tokens_out=130, seconds=1.4,
                  tok_per_doc=420, sec_per_doc=0.28),
    "build": dict(in_rate=3.00, out_rate=15.00, tokens_in=68_000, tokens_out=9_000, seconds=0.0),
}


def call_cost(kind: str, calls: int = 1, docs: int = 1) -> float:
    """Priced from the declared rates. `docs` is how much evidence the call carries.

    A judgment handed six excerpts is a bigger prompt than one handed a single
    register entry, and that difference is the whole point of scoping retrieval.
    """
    r = RATES[kind]
    extra = max(0, docs - 1) * r.get("tok_per_doc", 0)
    per = ((r["tokens_in"] + extra) * r["in_rate"]
           + r["tokens_out"] * r["out_rate"]) / 1e6
    return round(per * calls, 6)


def call_seconds(kind: str, calls: int = 1, docs: int = 1) -> float:
    r = RATES[kind]
    extra = max(0, docs - 1) * r.get("sec_per_doc", 0.0)
    return round((r["seconds"] + extra) * calls, 2)


def frontier_cost() -> float:
    return call_cost("frontier")


def build_cost(tokens_in: int, tokens_out: int) -> float:
    r = RATES["build"]
    return round((tokens_in * r["in_rate"] + tokens_out * r["out_rate"]) / 1e6, 4)

FILE: corpus.py
================================================
"""
jig.corpus — a frozen, dated source corpus.

Every judgment is answered from here, and every citation points into here. Two
properties matter for the experiment:

  * documents are versioned and carry effective dates, so "the rule changed" is a
    real event in the data rather than a caveat in a footnote;
  * retrieval is bounded — a predicate may only read the sources its program
    names, which is what makes "the evidence for this predicate is missing" a
    finding rather than a shrug.

The corpus is synthetic. It is a fixture for measuring the pipeline, not a
description of any real organisation's policy.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import copy
import math
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from .core import Excerpt

STOP = {
    "the", "a", "an", "of", "to", "for", "and", "or", "in", "on", "at", "by",
    "is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "with", "as", "that", "this",
    "it", "its", "if", "not", "may", "must", "any", "all", "we", "our", "can",
    "do", "does", "should", "would", "which", "who", "when", "what", "how",
}

WORD = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9][a-z0-9$.,'\-/]*")


def words(text: str) -> list[str]:
    return [w.strip(".,'") for w in WORD.findall(text.lower()) if w not in STOP]


@dataclass
class Doc:
    id: str
    title: str
    kind: str                  # policy | registry | memo | contract | calendar
    date: str                  # last updated
    effective: str | None      # when this version takes effect
    supersedes: str | None
    text: str
    tags: tuple = ()

    def sentences(self) -> list[str]:
        parts = re.split(r"(?<=[.!?])\s+|\n+", self.text)
        return [p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip()]

    def public(self, snippet: bool = True) -> dict:
        return {"id": self.id, "title": self.title, "kind": self.kind,
                "date": self.date, "effective": self.effective,
                "supersedes": self.supersedes,
                "text": self.text if not snippet else self.text[:400],
                "tags": list(self.tags)}


def _d(*a, **k) -> Doc:
    return Doc(*a, **k)

       ("contract", "msa", "kestrel")),
