class Corpus:
    """A frozen corpus. Copy it to stage an intervention."""

    def __init__(self, docs: list[Doc] | None = None):
        self.docs: dict[str, Doc] = {d.id: d for d in (docs or DOCS)}
        self._df: dict[str, int] = {}
        self._rebuild()

    def _rebuild(self) -> None:
        self._df = {}
        for d in self.docs.values():
            for w in set(words(d.title + " " + d.text + " " + d.id)):
                self._df[w] = self._df.get(w, 0) + 1
        self._n = max(1, len(self.docs))

    # -- staging -----------------------------------------------------------

    def clone(self) -> "Corpus":
        return Corpus([copy.deepcopy(d) for d in self.docs.values()])

    def remove(self, doc_id: str) -> "Corpus":
        c = self.clone()
        c.docs.pop(doc_id, None)
        c._rebuild()
        return c

    def replace_text(self, doc_id: str, old: str, new: str) -> "Corpus":
        c = self.clone()
        if doc_id in c.docs:
            c.docs[doc_id].text = c.docs[doc_id].text.replace(old, new)
        c._rebuild()
        return c

    def add(self, doc: Doc) -> "Corpus":
        c = self.clone()
        c.docs[doc.id] = doc
        c._rebuild()
        return c

    # -- lookup ------------------------------------------------------------

    def get(self, doc_id: str) -> Doc | None:
        return self.docs.get(doc_id)

    def by_prefix(self, prefix: str, as_of: str | None = None) -> list[Doc]:
        out = [d for d in self.docs.values() if d.id.startswith(prefix)]
        if as_of:
            out = [d for d in out if (d.effective or "0000") <= as_of]
        return sorted(out, key=lambda d: (d.effective or d.date, d.id))

    def in_force(self, prefix: str, as_of: str) -> Doc | None:
        """The version of a document family that governs on `as_of`."""
        docs = self.by_prefix(prefix, as_of)
        return docs[-1] if docs else None

    def is_superseded(self, doc: Doc, as_of: str) -> bool:
        for other in self.docs.values():
            if other.supersedes == doc.id and (other.effective or "9999") <= as_of:
                return True
        return False

    # -- retrieval ---------------------------------------------------------

    def _idf(self, w: str) -> float:
        df = self._df.get(w, 0)
        return math.log(1 + (self._n - df + 0.5) / (df + 0.5))

    def score(self, doc: Doc, terms: list[str]) -> float:
        body = words(doc.title + " " + doc.text + " " + doc.id)
        if not body:
            return 0.0
        counts: dict[str, int] = {}
        for w in body:
            counts[w] = counts.get(w, 0) + 1
        s = 0.0
        for t in terms:
            if t in counts:
                s += self._idf(t) * (counts[t] / (counts[t] + 1.6))
        # small bonus for exact phrase presence
        joined = " ".join(body)
        if len(terms) >= 2 and " ".join(terms) in joined:
            s += 1.5
        return s

    def search(self, query: str, as_of: str, only: list[str] | None = None,
               k: int = 4) -> list["Excerpt"]:
        """Bounded search. `only` is the source scope the program permits."""
        terms = words(query)
        pool = list(self.docs.values())
        if only:
            pool = [d for d in pool if _match_scope(d, only)]
        ranked = []
        for d in pool:
            s = self.score(d, terms)
            if s > 0:
                ranked.append((s, d))
        ranked.sort(key=lambda x: (-x[0], x[1].id))
        out = []
        for s, d in ranked[:k]:
            out.append(Excerpt(
                doc_id=d.id, title=d.title, date=d.date,
                text=d.text, quote=quote_for(d, terms),
                score=s, effective=d.effective,
                superseded=self.is_superseded(d, as_of)))
        return out

    def public(self) -> list[dict]:
        return [d.public() for d in sorted(self.docs.values(), key=lambda x: x.id)]


def _match_scope(doc: Doc, only: list[str]) -> bool:
    for pat in only:
        if pat.endswith("/") and doc.id.startswith(pat):
            return True
        if doc.id == pat or doc.kind == pat:
            return True
        if pat.startswith("tag:") and pat[4:] in doc.tags:
            return True
    return False


def quote_for(doc: Doc, terms: list[str], max_sentences: int = 2) -> str:
    """The verbatim sentence(s) a citation should point at."""
    sents = doc.sentences()
    if not sents:
        return ""
    scored = sorted(
        ((sum(1 for t in terms if t in words(s)), -i, s) for i, s in enumerate(sents)),
        key=lambda x: (-x[0], -x[1]))
    best = [scored[0]] if scored else []
    for cand in scored[1:]:
        if cand[0] > 0 and len(best) < max_sentences:
            best.append(cand)
    order = sorted(best, key=lambda x: -x[1])
    return " ".join(s for _, _, s in order).strip()


DEFAULT = Corpus()

FILE: demo.py

"""
jig.demo — the whole argument, in one terminal run.

    python3 -m jig.demo
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import textwrap
import time

from .bench import economics, run_all
from .corpus import DEFAULT
from .core import frontier_cost
from .engine import Engine
from .programs import LABELLED, PROPOSAL_V2, default_library
from .reuse import experiment as reuse_experiment
from .verify import apply_proposal, program_interventions, run_intervention, verify_program

W = 96


def rule(ch: str = "─", n: int = W) -> str:
    return ch * n


def h(n: int, title: str) -> None:
    print(f"\n\033[1m{n:02d}  {title}\033[0m")
    print(rule())


def wrap(text: str, indent: int = 4) -> str:
    return textwrap.fill(" ".join(text.split()), width=W,
                         initial_indent=" " * indent,
                         subsequent_indent=" " * indent)


def chip(answer: str) -> str:
    return {"yes": "\033[32myes\033[0m", "no": "\033[31mno\033[0m",
            "unknown": "\033[33munknown\033[0m", "n/a": "\033[2mn/a\033[0m"}.get(
                answer, answer)


def main() -> None:
    t0 = time.perf_counter()
    eng = Engine(library=default_library(), corpus=DEFAULT)
    v1 = eng.library.get("PROC-01", "1.0")

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 1
    h(1, "A question, and the thing it actually depends on")
    q1 = next(q for q in LABELLED if q["id"] == "q01")["question"]
    print(wrap(q1))
    print(wrap("A model can answer this in one pass. The claim worth testing is "
               "narrower: that the judgments this answer depends on can be named, "
               "typed, and reused on the next question — and that we can tell the "
               "difference between a decision that is right and a decision that is "
               "right for the wrong reason.", 4))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 2
    h(2, "The program: predicates, sources, and what happens when a source is gone")
    p = v1.public()
    print(f"    {p['id']} @ {p['version']}  ·  {p['title']}")
    print(f"    choose: {p['combine_pretty']}")
    print(f"      → {p['labels']['yes']} / {p['labels']['no']} / "
          f"{p['labels']['unknown']} / {p['labels']['escalate']}\n")
    for x in p["predicates"]:
        print(f"    {x['id']}  {x['ask']}")
        print(f"        reads {x['requires']}  ·  if missing → {x['on_missing']}"
              f"  ·  {x['kind']}")
    print(wrap("That structure is the reusable object. It costs one frontier-scale "
               "authoring pass: $0.339 for this program.", 4))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 3
    h(3, "Resolving it: only what can still change the answer")
    d = eng.run(q1)
    print(f"    {d.label}   ({d.program_id} @ {d.program_version}, as of {d.as_of})")
    print(f"    {d.evaluated} predicates resolved, {len(d.skipped)} skipped, "
          f"${d.cost['total']:.6f}, {d.latency_ms/1000:.1f} s, "
          f"{d.cost['judge_calls']} judge call(s)\n")
    for f in d.findings:
        print(f"    {f.predicate}  {chip(f.answer):<22} {f.rationale}")
        for c in f.citations:
            print(f"          ↳ \033[2m{c.doc_id}\033[0m \"{c.quote[:74]}\"")
    if d.skipped:
        print()
        for s in d.skipped:
            print(f"    skipped {s['predicate']}: {s['reason']}")

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 4
    h(4, "Two ways the structure earns its keep")
    d3 = eng.run(next(q for q in LABELLED if q["id"] == "q03")["question"])
    skipped = [s["predicate"] for s in d3.skipped]
    print(f"    over the limit  → {d3.label}: {d3.evaluated} predicates resolved, "
          f"skipped {skipped}")
    print(wrap("The limit fails, so the remaining exact predicates "
               f"({', '.join(skipped)}) cannot change the outcome and are never "
               "retrieved for. The one thing still worth resolving is whether an "
               "exception pre-approves the request."))
    d5 = eng.run(next(q for q in LABELLED if q["id"] == "q05")["question"])
    print(f"\n    frozen budget   → {d5.label}")
    for f in d5.findings:
        print(f"      {f.predicate}  {chip(f.answer):<22} {f.rationale[:90]}")
    print(wrap("The budget predicate fails and the purchase is approved anyway, on "
               "a memo that pre-approves conference travel plus a calendar entry "
               "showing the event. Two documents, one judgment, and a single-pass "
               "pipeline has no reason to look for either."))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 5
    h(5, "Refusing: a gap, and a question outside the library")
    d9 = eng.run(next(q for q in LABELLED if q["id"] == "q09")["question"])
    print(f"    {d9.label}  — {d9.gaps[0]}")
    d10 = eng.run(next(q for q in LABELLED if q["id"] == "q10")["question"])
    print(f"    {d10.label} — {d10.gaps[0]}")
    print(wrap("Neither of these is a failure to answer. They are the answers: one "
               f"predicate is unsettled, and nothing in the library covers the "
               f"question. Escalating costs a frontier call (${frontier_cost():.4f}) "
               "and is counted as one."))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 6
    h(6, "The disagreement: v1.0 is confidently wrong on one question")
    q2 = next(q for q in LABELLED if q["id"] == "q02")["question"]
    d2 = eng.run(q2)
    print(wrap(q2))
    print(f"    {d2.label} — {d2.findings[0].rationale}")
    print(wrap("Nimbus Hosting's security review expired in 2024. Policy v2.0 §5 "
               "requires a current review for any vendor that processes personal "
               "data, and hosting does. v1.0 has no predicate for it, so the "
               "condition cannot influence the decision. On the labelled set that "
               "is one wrong answer out of nine — easy to miss."))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 7
    h(7, "Which is why the tests are interventions, not agreements")
    res = verify_program(eng, v1)
    print(f"    PROC-01 v1.0: {res['interventions_passed']}/{res['interventions_total']} "
          f"interventions, {res['regression_passed']}/{res['regression_total']} labelled\n")
    for iv in res["interventions"]:
        mark = "\033[32mPASS\033[0m" if iv["pass"] else "\033[31mFAIL\033[0m"
        print(f"    {mark}  {iv['id']:<18} {iv['kind']:<32} expected "
              f"{iv['expect']:<22} got {iv['got']}")
        if not iv["pass"] and iv["detail"]:
            print(f"          {iv['detail']}")
    missing = next(i for i in res["interventions"] if i["id"] == "iv-missing-01")
    print()
    print(wrap("Delete the security review register and v1.0 keeps approving. A "
               "program with no predicate for a source cannot notice the source is "
               "gone — and no amount of agreement with a frontier answer exposes "
               "that. This is the failure mode the intervention suite exists for."))
    print(wrap(missing["rationale"]))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 8
    h(8, "The second role: propose, then be verified")
    prop = PROPOSAL_V2
    print(f"    {prop['id']} → PROC-01 v{prop['proposed_version']}: {prop['title']}")
    print(wrap(prop["motivation"]))
    print(f"\n    {prop['patch']['combine_before']}")
    print(f"    {prop['patch']['combine_after']}")
    out = apply_proposal(eng, prop)
    v = out["verification"]
    print(f"\n    candidate: {v['interventions_passed']}/{v['interventions_total']} "
          f"interventions, {v['regression_passed']}/{v['regression_total']} labelled "
          f"→ \033[1m{out['status']}\033[0m (active: PROC-01 "
          f"v{eng.library.get('PROC-01').version})")
    print(wrap("A proposal does not edit the library. It creates a candidate, and "
               "the candidate has to pass the same suite the old version failed, "
               "without regressing anything it already got right."))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 9
    h(9, "Four pipelines, same corpus, same axis")
    arms = run_all(eng)
    print(f"    {'arm':<4}{'correct':>9}{'material':>10}{'overreach':>11}"
          f"{'defer':>7}{'esc':>5}{'cited':>7}{'cost/dec':>12}{'latency':>10}")
    for k in ("A", "B1", "B2", "B3"):
        s = arms[k]["score"]
        print(f"    {k:<4}{s['correct']:>6}/{s['n']:<2}{s['material_errors']:>10}"
              f"{s['overreach']:>11}{s['deferred']:>7}{s['escalated']:>5}"
              f"{s['citation_support']*100:>6.0f}%{s['avg_cost_answered']:>12.6f}"
              f"{s['avg_latency_answered_ms']/1000:>9.1f}s")
    print()
    print("    (A's citation rate is not observable from one open-ended call, so it "
          "is excluded rather than scored zero.)\n")
    print(wrap("Arm A is modelled: its answers are the labelled ground truth, "
               "standing in for a real frontier run, and its cost and latency come "
               "from declared token rates. B1 through B3 are executed. B2 and B3 "
               "agree with A on this set; the difference between them is work done, "
               "not correctness — 4.1 predicates resolved versus 3.2."))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 10
    h(10, "Pay once, refresh the facts")
    econ = economics(eng, arms)
    ex = reuse_experiment(eng, econ)
    print(f"    one-time build      ${econ['one_time_build_cost']:.4f} "
          f"({econ['programs']} programs, {econ['versions']} versions)")
    print(f"    per decision        ${econ['reused_per_question']:.6f}  vs frontier "
          f"${econ['frontier_per_question']:.4f}  → {econ['ratio']}×")
    print(f"    break-even          {econ['break_even_questions']} questions")
    print(f"    held-out questions  ${ex['held_out_cost_per_answered']:.6f} each "
          f"({ex['held_out_ratio']}× frontier) — the author never saw them\n")
    for var in ex["variants"]:
        ch = ", ".join(f"{c['id']} {c['before']}→{c['after']}" for c in var["changed"]) \
            or "no verdict moved"
        mark = "ok  " if var["pass"] else "FAIL"
        print(f"    {mark} {var['title']}")
        print(f"         {ch}")
    print(wrap("Every one of those is a corpus edit or a date change. No program was "
               "rebuilt, no predicate was rewritten, and the cost per decision did "
               "not move. That is what makes the structure — not the answers — the "
               "thing worth accumulating."))

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- 11
    h(11, "What this does not show")
    for line in [
        "The corpus is synthetic, and the labelled set is fifteen questions. Every "
        "ground truth is derivable from the documents by hand, which is the point at "
        "this stage and far too small to estimate an error rate.",
        "The judge is an offline entailment evaluator, not a model. The programs do "
        "not change when a model is plugged in — LLMJudge sends exactly the retrieved "
        "excerpts — but no model was run here.",
        "The frontier arm is a stand-in priced at declared rates. Treat that column as "
        "the cost of the alternative, not as a measurement of any particular model.",
        "Registers are read as current state. Versioned policies are resolved by the "
        "date in force; a register is a point-in-time record and would need "
        "snapshots in a real deployment.",
    ]:
        print(wrap("· " + line, 4))

    dt = time.perf_counter() - t0
    print(f"\n{rule('═')}")
    print(f"  {len(LABELLED)} questions · {len(DEFAULT.docs)} documents · "
          f"{sum(len(v) for v in eng.library.versions.values())} program versions · "
          f"{len(program_interventions('PROC-01')) + len(program_interventions('DPA-01')) + len(program_interventions('EMERG-01'))} interventions · "
          f"run in {dt:.2f} s")
    print(rule("═"))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()



================================================
FILE: DESIGN.md
================================================
# Design notes: requirement → implementation

This maps each part of the original idea onto the code that implements it, so the build can
be checked against the ask rather than against its own summary.

---

## 1. The decision program

> *"Choose A when P1 and P2 hold and P3 does not apply" — each predicate gets the specific
> knowledge it needs, and the program says how the judgments combine.*

`core.Program` + `core.Predicate` (`jig/core.py`).

| Element | Field | Shown as |
|---|---|---|
| the question it answers | `question_template`, `slots` | the bound slots in the decision header |
| judgments | `predicates[].ask` | predicate ledger |
| possible answers | `yes_label` / `no_label` / `unknown_label` / `escalate_label` | verdict card; mapped to YES/NO/DEFER/ESCALATE for cross-arm scoring via `Program.canon` |
| knowledge required | `predicates[].requires` | "reads:" in the ledger, and the retrieval scope in the trace |
| what happens if missing | `predicates[].on_missing` (`no` \| `unknown` \| `escalate`) + `applies_if` | "if missing:" in the ledger |
| combining logic | `combine`, parsed by `parse_expr` into a 3-valued tree | the `choose:` line |
| how it was validated | `tests`, `verified`, `verification`, `lineage` | verification badges, before/after table |

Three-valued logic (Kleene) is not decoration: `UNKNOWN` has to survive `AND`/`OR` without
being silently coerced to false, or `DEFER` becomes impossible to express. `UNKNOWN OR TRUE`
is TRUE (an exception settles it regardless of the missing fact); `UNKNOWN AND FALSE` is
FALSE (the missing fact cannot matter).

## 2. Predicate-level behaviours

| Requirement | Where |
|---|---|
| exact lookup predicates evaluated in code | `judge.EXACT_OPS` — threshold, register status, date arithmetic, budget arithmetic, taxonomy, contract lookup. They see **only** the excerpts retrieval returned for that predicate, so the evidence path is the same one the citation shows. |
| judge predicates over an excerpt | `judge.JUDGE_OPS` — the exception judgment and the "is this a service" judgment. `LLMJudge` is the same interface wired to an OpenAI-compatible endpoint. |
| a separate search for exceptions | `P6` in `PROC-01`, scoped to `memo/` and the events calendar; it is evaluated **last**, because it can only matter when the main conjunction has failed. |
| stop early if a decisive predicate fails | `engine.Engine.run` breaks the moment the combining rule is decided. For `q03` the failed limit decides it and `P2,P3,P4,P5` are never fetched. |
| report a precise open question when a later predicate is unknown | `q09` returns `DEFER` with *"vendor register has no entry for Halcyon Data — request an updated register"*, surfaced as Open questions. |
| two co-dependent predicates get joint evidence | `_judge_exception` receives the memo **and** the calendar together and cites both; the calendar alone cannot settle it and the memo alone cannot confirm the event was approved. |
| ensemble: one local model per predicate, specialist only where it wins | predicates declare `kind` (`exact` / `judge` / `exception`). Exact ones never call a model; only judge-kind predicates do. Call counts are reported per decision. |

## 3. Gap-directed retrieval

`Engine._matters` answers "can this predicate still change the outcome?" by enumerating the
remaining unknowns (six predicates → at most 64 completions) and checking whether flipping
the predicate changes the result under any of them. This is stricter than it looks: the naive
version — flip it while everything else is `UNKNOWN` — treats two `UNKNOWN` results as
identical and skips predicates that actually matter, which is how the first implementation
silently produced `DEFER` for everything.

Consequences visible in the trace and the tests:

- `q03` (over the limit): 2 predicates resolved, three skipped, only the exception searched.
- `q12` (hardware onboarding): `NOT P1` is already true, so `P2` — the contract search — is
  never performed. One predicate, one citation.
- Retrieval is **scoped**: `corpus.search(..., only=pred.requires)`. A predicate cannot widen
  its own search; when its scope has nothing, it reports a gap.
- Evidence *against* the leading answer is what the exception predicate is for: on `q05` the
  budget predicate fails and the run continues specifically to look for a reason the answer
  could still be yes.
- The loop stops when resolved, and the skipped predicates are listed with the reason.

## 4. The frontier model's second role

`programs.PROPOSAL_V2` + `verify.apply_proposal`.

1. Run the small path; where it disagrees with a verified answer, show the frontier model the
   case and ask what is missing (predicate, exception, source requirement, decomposition).
2. The proposal is data, not an edit: it names the predicates to add and the combining rule
   before and after.
3. A candidate program is built from it and run through the **same** intervention suite plus
   the labelled regression set.
4. Admitted only on a clean pass; recorded in `verification` with counts; the rejected version
   stays in the library for the before/after view.

Nothing in the admission path consults the proposal's reasoning. If the candidate fails, it is
rejected regardless of how convincing the motivation reads — `apply_proposal` returns
`{"status": "rejected", "verification": ...}` in that case. (Try it: edit `PROC_V2.combine` to
drop `(NOT P3 OR P4)` and re-run the verification.)

## 5. Interventions instead of agreement

`verify.INTERVENTIONS` — five kinds, twelve tests:

| kind | what it proves | example |
|---|---|---|
| `flip_decisive_fact` | the decision is actually *about* the fact | suspend the vendor → DENY |
| `irrelevant_rewording` | the decision is not about the wording | add meeting-note chatter → same verdict **and the same citation set** |
| `remove_indispensable_evidence` | the pipeline knows when it cannot decide | delete the review register → DEFER |
| `governing_rule_changed` | the rule in force is used, not the newest file | same question dated 2026-05-01 → v1.4 limit |
| `out_of_scope` | routing can decline | office lease → ESCALATE |

Values are not compared with the frontier at all. The comparison arms exist to price the
alternative and to localise failures, which is why agreement is reported last and labelled as
weak.

## 6. The comparison arms

`bench.py`. Same 15 questions, same frozen corpus, same output contract, labels projected onto
one axis. B1 is the shape of "attach the sources and ask" — one retrieval, no decomposition,
no missing-knowledge policy, no scope check. It is worse in the specific ways the idea
predicts: it misses the exception memo (`q05`), applies the newest policy to an old date
(`q07`), answers instead of deferring (`q09`) and instead of escalating (`q10`).

Failures are localised, not totalled: rerun with the program pinned (decomposition?), then
with the decisive document handed over (retrieval?). Everything so far localises to
`decomposition` or `scope` — no case survives both injections, i.e. no pure operator failure
on this set. That is a statement about this fixture, not a general result.

## 7. What would make this claim wrong

Recorded here so the build is falsifiable rather than self-congratulatory:

- If per-decision cost did not stay low on held-out questions → reuse is illusory. Measured:
  $0.000396 on 10 unseen questions vs $0.000404 on the authored ones.
- If a fact change moved verdicts without a rebuild *for the wrong reason* (e.g. a cached
  answer) → the variant suite would show the wrong question moving. Each variant pins which
  question must move.
- If the intervention suite passed for a program that could not defer → v1.0 is the control:
  it fails exactly there.
- If the small path only matched the frontier by luck → B1's failure profile shows the
  structure is doing the work, not the retrieval.
- If the numbers were an artefact of a friendly corpus → the limitation is stated: 15
  questions, one domain, no error-rate claim.



================================================
FILE: engine.py
================================================
"""
jig.engine — plan, bind, resolve, combine.

The loop is built around one question at every step: *which unresolved predicate
could still change the outcome?* Predicates that cannot are skipped rather than
retrieved for, and the run stops the moment the combining rule is decided. That
is where the cost saving comes from, and it is visible in the trace: a request
that fails the limit check resolves the exception and never touches the vendor,
security or budget sources.

Reuse lives in `bind`: the program is selected from the library, its slots are
filled from the new question, and its source scope and missing-knowledge policy
carry over unchanged. Only the facts are new.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import itertools
import re
import time

from . import core
from .core import (Decision, Finding, Excerpt, Program, TRUE, FALSE, UNKNOWN,
                   call_cost, call_seconds)
from .corpus import Corpus, DEFAULT
from .judge import get_judge, ReferenceJudge
from .programs import Library, default_library

ISO = re.compile(r"\b(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})\b")
MONEY = re.compile(r"\$\s?([\d][\d,]*(?:\.\d+)?)")
BUDGET_CODE = re.compile(r"\b([A-Z]{2,}-[A-Z0-9]{2,})\b")

CATEGORIES = ["conference travel", "office supplies", "analytics", "hosting",
              "payroll", "support", "hardware", "software", "crm", "services"]

DEFAULT_TODAY = "2026-11-05"

# A predicate only applies when its subject exists in the request. When it does
# not, the predicate is vacuous-true: it cannot block a decision that does not
# involve its subject, and it costs nothing to establish that.
CONDITIONS = {
    "has_vendor": lambda ctx: bool(ctx.get("vendor")),
    "has_budget_line": lambda ctx: bool(ctx.get("budget_line")),
    "has_event": lambda ctx: bool(ctx.get("event")),
    "always": lambda ctx: True,
}


class Plan:
    def __init__(self, program: Program | None, slots: dict, as_of: str,
                 routed: bool, reason: str, scores: dict | None = None):
        self.program = program
        self.slots = slots
        self.as_of = as_of
        self.routed = routed
        self.reason = reason
        self.scores = scores or {}

    def public(self) -> dict:
        return {"program": self.program.id if self.program else None,
                "version": self.program.version if self.program else None,
                "slots": self.slots, "as_of": self.as_of,
                "routed": self.routed, "reason": self.reason,
                "scores": self.scores}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# slot grounding
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def extract_slots(question: str, corpus: Corpus) -> dict:
    q = question
    low = q.lower()
    slots: dict = {"description": question.strip()}

    m = MONEY.search(q)
    if m:
        slots["amount"] = float(m.group(1).replace(",", ""))

    d = ISO.search(q)
    slots["as_of"] = d.group(1) if d else DEFAULT_TODAY

    b = BUDGET_CODE.search(q)
    if b and not ISO.fullmatch(b.group(1)):
        slots["budget_line"] = b.group(1)

    vend = re.search(r"\bfrom ([A-Z][\w&.\-]*(?: [A-Z][\w&.\-]*){0,3})", q)
    if not vend:
        vend = re.search(r"\bonboard(?:ing)? ([A-Z][\w&.\-]*(?: [A-Z][\w&.\-]*){0,3})"
                         r"(?: for| on| as of|,|$|\?)", q)
    if vend:
        slots["vendor"] = vend.group(1).strip()

    best = None
    for c in CATEGORIES:
        if c in low and (best is None or len(c) > len(best)):
            best = c
    if best:
        slots["category"] = best

    cal = corpus.get("events/calendar")
    if cal:
        for line in cal.text.splitlines():
            name = line.split("—")[0].strip()
            if name and name.lower() in low:
                slots["event"] = name
                break

    if re.search(r"cfo[^.]*notified[^.]*:\s*no", low) or "not notified" in low:
        slots["cfo_notified"] = False
    elif re.search(r"cfo[^.]*notified[^.]*:\s*yes", low) or \
            "notified within 24 hours" in low:
        slots["cfo_notified"] = True

    return slots


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# planner
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

class Engine:
    def __init__(self, library: Library | None = None,
                 corpus: Corpus | None = None,
                 judge: ReferenceJudge | None = None):
        self.library = library or default_library()
        self.corpus = corpus or DEFAULT
        self.judge = judge or get_judge()

    # -- routing -----------------------------------------------------------

    def plan(self, question: str, corpus: Corpus | None = None,
             program_id: str | None = None,
             version: str | None = None,
             program: Program | None = None) -> Plan:
        corpus = corpus or self.corpus
        slots = extract_slots(question, corpus)
        as_of = slots["as_of"]
        low = question.lower()

        if program is not None:
            return Plan(program, slots, as_of, True,
                        f"program {program.id}@{program.version} supplied directly "
                        "(candidate or pinned version)")

        if program_id:
            prog = self.library.get(program_id, version)
            if prog is None:
                return Plan(None, slots, as_of, False, f"no program {program_id}")
            return Plan(prog, slots, as_of, True,
                        f"program {program_id}@{prog.version} selected explicitly")

        scores: dict[str, int] = {}
        for prog in self.library.all_active():
            s = sum(1 for k in prog.route_keywords if k in low)
            missing = [r for r in prog.route_requires if r not in slots]
            if missing:
                s -= 2 * len(missing)
            scores[prog.id] = s

        if not scores:
            return Plan(None, slots, as_of, False, "the library is empty", scores)
        best_id = max(scores, key=lambda k: (scores[k], k))
        best = scores[best_id]
        if best < 1:
            return Plan(None, slots, as_of, False,
                        "no program in the library covers this question "
                        f"(best route score {best})", scores)
        prog = self.library.get(best_id)
        return Plan(prog, slots, as_of, True,
                    f"routed to {best_id}@{prog.version} (score {best})", scores)

    # -- the loop ----------------------------------------------------------

    def _query_for(self, prog: Program, pred, ctx: dict) -> str:
        parts = [pred.ask]
        for key in ("vendor", "category", "budget_line", "event", "description"):
            if ctx.get(key):
                parts.append(str(ctx[key]))
        return " ".join(parts)

    def _matters(self, prog: Program, pid: str, values: dict) -> bool:
        """Can this predicate still change the outcome under some completion?

        Flipping it while the other unresolved predicates stay unknown is too
        weak a test: two UNKNOWN results look identical. So the remaining
        unknowns are enumerated. That is cheap here (six predicates) and it is
        the honest version of "only retrieve for what still separates the
        answers".
        """
        expr = prog.expr()
        if expr.evaluate(values) != UNKNOWN:
            return False
        if pid not in expr.preds():
            return False
        others = [p for p in dict.fromkeys(expr.preds())
                  if p != pid and p not in values]
        for bits in itertools.product((TRUE, FALSE), repeat=len(others)):
            base = dict(values)
            base.update(dict(zip(others, bits)))
            a = dict(base); a[pid] = TRUE
            b = dict(base); b[pid] = FALSE
            if expr.evaluate(a) != expr.evaluate(b):
                return True
        return False

    def run(self, question: str, program_id: str | None = None,
            version: str | None = None, corpus: Corpus | None = None,
            slots_override: dict | None = None, targeted: bool = True,
            program: Program | None = None,
            forced: list[str] | None = None, scoped: bool = True) -> Decision:
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        corpus = corpus or self.corpus
        plan = self.plan(question, corpus, program_id, version, program)
        slots = dict(plan.slots)
        if slots_override:
            slots.update(slots_override)
        as_of = slots.get("as_of", DEFAULT_TODAY)

        trace: list[dict] = [{"step": "plan", "plan": plan.public(),
                              "cost": call_cost("planner"),
                              "seconds": call_seconds("planner")}]

        if plan.program is None:
            trace.append({"step": "escalate", "reason": plan.reason})
            return Decision(
                question=question, program_id="", program_version="",
                slots=slots, as_of=as_of, verdict="ESCALATE",
                label="ESCALATE", findings=[], gaps=[plan.reason],
                citations=[], stopped_early=False, skipped=[],
                trace=trace,
                cost={"planner": call_cost("planner"), "judge": 0.0,
                      "frontier": core.frontier_cost(),
                      "total": round(call_cost("planner") + core.frontier_cost(), 5),
                      "escalated": True},
                latency_ms=call_seconds("planner") * 1000 +
                           call_seconds("frontier") * 1000)

        prog = plan.program
        expr = prog.expr()
        ctx = dict(slots)
        values: dict[str, str] = {}
        findings: list[Finding] = []
        gaps: list[str] = []
        skipped: list[dict] = []
        judge_calls = 0
        jcost = 0.0
        jsec = 0.0
        escalate = False
        stopped_early = False

        trace.append({"step": "bind", "program": prog.id, "version": prog.version,
                      "slots": slots, "as_of": as_of,
                      "reused": {"predicates": [p.id for p in prog.predicates],
                                 "combine": prog.combine,
                                 "source_scope": {p.id: p.requires
                                                  for p in prog.predicates}},
                      "note": "structure reused; only the facts are new"})

        order = sorted(prog.predicates,
                       key=lambda p: ({"exact": 0, "judge": 1, "exception": 2}
                                      .get(p.kind, 1), p.id))

        for pred in order:
            cond = pred.applies_if
            if cond and not CONDITIONS.get(cond, CONDITIONS["always"])(ctx):
                values[pred.id] = TRUE
                findings.append(Finding(
                    predicate=pred.id, answer="n/a", confidence=1.0, citations=[],
                    rationale=f"does not apply: the request has no "
                              f"{cond.replace('has_', '').replace('_', ' ')}",
                    evaluator="code"))
                trace.append({"step": "not_applicable", "predicate": pred.id,
                              "condition": cond,
                              "note": "treated as satisfied; nothing retrieved"})
                if expr.evaluate(values) != UNKNOWN:
                    stopped_early = True
                    break
                continue

            if targeted and not self._matters(prog, pred.id, values):
                skipped.append({
                    "predicate": pred.id,
                    "reason": ("the rule is already decided"
                               if expr.evaluate(values) != UNKNOWN
                               else "cannot change the outcome given what is known"),
                    "saved": "1 retrieval + 1 judgment"})
                trace.append({"step": "skip", "predicate": pred.id,
                              "reason": skipped[-1]["reason"]})
                continue

            query = self._query_for(prog, pred, ctx)
            excerpts = corpus.search(query, as_of,
                                     only=(pred.requires if scoped else None),
                                     k=(4 if scoped else 6))
            if forced:
                have = {e.doc_id for e in excerpts}
                for doc_id in forced:
                    doc = corpus.get(doc_id)
                    if doc and doc_id not in have:
                        excerpts.append(Excerpt(
                            doc_id=doc.id, title=doc.title, date=doc.date,
                            text=doc.text, quote=doc.sentences()[0] if doc.sentences() else "",
                            score=0.0, effective=doc.effective))
            res = self.judge.evaluate(pred, ctx, excerpts, corpus)
            is_model = pred.kind in ("judge", "exception")
            if is_model:
                judge_calls += 1
                jcost += call_cost("judge", 1, docs=max(1, len(excerpts)))
                jsec += call_seconds("judge", 1, docs=max(1, len(excerpts)))

            mapped = res.answer
            if res.answer == UNKNOWN:
                gap = res.gap or f"{pred.id} could not be resolved from the sources"
                gaps.append(gap)
                if pred.on_missing == "no":
                    mapped = FALSE
                elif pred.on_missing == "escalate":
                    escalate = True
                values[pred.id] = mapped
            else:
                values[pred.id] = mapped

            f = Finding(predicate=pred.id, answer=res.answer,
                        confidence=res.confidence, citations=res.citations,
                        rationale=res.rationale, evaluator=res.evaluator,
                        ms=res.ms, gap=res.gap, cost=res.cost)
            findings.append(f)
            trace.append({
                "step": "resolve", "predicate": pred.id, "ask": pred.ask,
                "query": query, "scope": pred.requires,
                "hits": [e.public() for e in excerpts],
                "answer": res.answer, "mapped": mapped,
                "evaluator": res.evaluator, "rationale": res.rationale,
                "gap": res.gap, "ms": round(res.ms, 2),
                "cost": (call_cost("judge", 1, docs=max(1, len(excerpts)))
                         if is_model else 0.0)})

            if expr.evaluate(values) != UNKNOWN:
                stopped_early = True
                trace.append({"step": "determined",
                              "expression": prog.combine,
                              "values": dict(values)})
                if targeted:
                    rest = [p.id for p in order
                            if p.id not in values and p.id not in
                            {s["predicate"] for s in skipped}]
                    for pid in rest:
                        skipped.append({"predicate": pid,
                                        "reason": "the rule is decided; not fetched",
                                        "saved": "1 retrieval + 1 judgment"})
                break

        value = expr.evaluate(values)
        if escalate:
            verdict, label = "ESCALATE", prog.escalate_label
        elif value == TRUE:
            verdict, label = "YES", prog.yes_label
        elif value == FALSE:
            verdict, label = "NO", prog.no_label
        else:
            verdict, label = "DEFER", prog.unknown_label

        seen: dict[str, Excerpt] = {}
        for f in findings:
            for c in f.citations:
                if c.doc_id not in seen:
                    seen[c.doc_id] = c

        planner_cost = call_cost("planner")
        total = round(planner_cost + jcost, 6)

        trace.append({"step": "combine", "expression": prog.combine,
                      "pretty": str(expr), "values": values,
                      "verdict": verdict, "label": label,
                      "gaps": gaps,
                      "economics": {"planner": planner_cost,
                                    "judge_calls": judge_calls,
                                    "judge": round(jcost, 5),
                                    "total": total,
                                    "frontier_equivalent": core.frontier_cost()}})

        return Decision(
            question=question, program_id=prog.id, program_version=prog.version,
            slots=slots, as_of=as_of, verdict=verdict, label=label,
            findings=findings, gaps=gaps, citations=list(seen.values()),
            stopped_early=stopped_early, skipped=skipped, trace=trace,
            cost={"planner": planner_cost, "judge": round(jcost, 6),
                  "judge_calls": judge_calls,
                  "frontier": core.frontier_cost(), "total": total,
                  "escalated": False},
            latency_ms=(call_seconds("planner") + jsec) * 1000,
            evaluated=len(findings))



================================================
FILE: judge.py
================================================
"""
jig.judge — who evaluates a predicate.

Two evaluators behind one interface:

  exact operators   deterministic code. Thresholds, register status, dates,
                    remaining budget. The program says which sources each one
                    may read, and the operator sees only the excerpts retrieval
                    returned for that predicate — it cannot reach around the
                    evidence and consult a private table.

  judge operators   a bounded judgment over supplied evidence, for the things
                    code should not be doing: does this memo pre-approve this
                    request, does this excerpt establish that a vendor handles
                    personal data, is this equipment safety-critical.

`ReferenceJudge` is the offline implementation of the judge role: entailment
patterns over the excerpts, deterministic and inspectable. `LLMJudge` is the
same interface wired to an OpenAI-compatible endpoint (a resident Qwen, say);
set JIG_LLM_BASE / JIG_LLM_KEY / JIG_LLM_MODEL and every judge predicate goes to
that model instead. The programs do not change.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import time
import urllib.request

from .core import UNKNOWN, TRUE, FALSE, Predicate, Excerpt

MONEY = re.compile(r"\$\s?([\d][\d,]*(?:\.\d+)?)")
ISO = re.compile(r"\b(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})\b")


def money(s: str | None) -> float | None:
    if not s:
        return None
    m = MONEY.search(s)
    return float(m.group(1).replace(",", "")) if m else None


def months_between(a: str, b: str) -> float:
    ya, ma, da = (int(x) for x in a.split("-"))
    yb, mb, db = (int(x) for x in b.split("-"))
    return (yb - ya) * 12 + (mb - ma) + (db - da) / 31.0


def pick(excerpts: list[Excerpt], prefix: str, as_of: str) -> Excerpt | None:
    """The in-force excerpt from a document family.

    A version that takes effect after the request date does not govern it, even
    if retrieval returned it. Getting this wrong is the quiet way a pipeline
    applies next quarter's rule to last quarter's purchase.
    """
    cands = [e for e in excerpts if e.doc_id.startswith(prefix)]
    if not cands:
        return None
    dated = [e for e in cands if e.effective]
    if dated:
        # a versioned document family: only versions in force on the date count
        in_force = [e for e in dated if e.effective <= as_of]
        if not in_force:
            return None
        in_force.sort(key=lambda e: (e.effective, e.doc_id))
        return in_force[-1]
    # a register or a memo: a current-state record, not a versioned rule. Its
    # last-updated date is reported with the citation, but it is not used to
    # suppress the record for earlier questions.
    cands.sort(key=lambda e: (e.date, e.doc_id))
    return cands[-1]


def line_for(text: str, needle: str) -> str | None:
    n = needle.lower().strip()
    for line in re.split(r"\n+", text):
        if n and n in line.lower():
            return line.strip()
    return None


class Result:
    def __init__(self, answer: str, confidence: float, rationale: str,
                 citations: list[Excerpt], gap: str | None = None,
                 evaluator: str = "code", cost: float = 0.0):
        self.answer = answer
        self.confidence = confidence
        self.rationale = rationale
        self.citations = citations
        self.gap = gap
        self.evaluator = evaluator
        self.cost = cost


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# exact operators: code
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def op_manager_limit(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    as_of = ctx["as_of"]
    doc = pick(ex, "policy/procurement", as_of)
    if doc is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "no procurement policy retrieved", [],
                      "procurement policy for this date is not in the corpus")
    m = re.search(r"up to \$([\d,]+) may be approved by the requestor's manager",
                  doc.text)
    if not m:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "limit clause not found in policy text", [doc],
                      "manager limit clause missing from the policy version")
    limit = float(m.group(1).replace(",", ""))
    amount = ctx.get("amount")
    if amount is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "the request states no amount", [doc],
                      "the request does not state a total value")
    ok = amount <= limit
    return Result(
        TRUE if ok else FALSE, 1.0,
        f"amount ${amount:,.0f} is {'within' if ok else 'above'} the manager "
        f"limit ${limit:,.0f} in force on {as_of} ({doc.doc_id})",
        [doc])


def op_vendor_status(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    vendor = ctx["vendor"]
    doc = pick(ex, "registry/vendors", ctx["as_of"])
    if doc is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "vendor register not retrieved", [],
                      "vendor register is not in the corpus")
    line = line_for(doc.text, vendor)
    if not line:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, f"{vendor} has no entry in the register", [doc],
                      f"vendor register has no entry for {vendor} — request an "
                      "updated register")
    m = re.search(r"status:\s*([a-z]+)", line)
    status = (m.group(1) if m else "unknown").lower()
    ok = status == "approved"
    return Result(TRUE if ok else FALSE, 1.0,
                  f"{vendor} status in the register is '{status}'", [doc],
                  None if ok else None)


def op_security_review(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    vendor = ctx["vendor"]
    as_of = ctx["as_of"]
    doc = pick(ex, "registry/security", as_of)
    if doc is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "security review register not retrieved", [],
                      "security review register is not available")
    line = line_for(doc.text, vendor)
    if not line:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, f"no review recorded for {vendor}", [doc],
                      f"security review register has no review for {vendor}")
    status = "expired" if "expired" in line.lower() else "current"
    dm = ISO.search(line)
    if not dm:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "review line has no date", [doc],
                      "security review entry has no completion date")
    reviewed = dm.group(1)
    age = months_between(reviewed, as_of)
    fresh = status == "current" and age <= 12.0
    return Result(TRUE if fresh else FALSE, 1.0,
                  f"{vendor} review completed {reviewed} ({age:.1f} months before "
                  f"{as_of}), status {status}", [doc])


def op_budget_line(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    line_code = ctx.get("budget_line")
    if not line_code:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "no budget line named", [],
                      "the request does not name a budget line")
    doc = pick(ex, "registry/budget", ctx["as_of"])
    if doc is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "budget register not retrieved", [],
                      "budget register is not available")
    line = line_for(doc.text, line_code)
    if not line:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, f"{line_code} not found", [doc],
                      f"budget line {line_code} is not in the register")
    remaining = money(line)
    status = "open"
    for s in ("frozen", "closed"):
        if s in line.lower():
            status = s
    amount = ctx.get("amount")
    if amount is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, f"{line_code}: no amount to test against",
                      [doc], "the request does not state a total value")
    ok = status == "open" and remaining is not None and remaining >= amount
    why = (f"{line_code}: ${remaining:,.0f} remaining, status {status}"
           if remaining is not None else f"{line_code}: no remaining figure")
    return Result(TRUE if ok else FALSE, 1.0, why, [doc])


def op_personal_data(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    cat = (ctx.get("category") or "").lower()
    doc = pick(ex, "policy/data-taxonomy", ctx["as_of"])
    if not cat:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "the request states no spend category", [],
                      "the request does not state a spend category, so it cannot "
                      "be classified against the data taxonomy")
    if doc is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "no taxonomy retrieved", [],
                      "data classification policy is not available")
    low = doc.text.lower()
    yes_part = low.split("categories that do not process personal data")[0]
    no_part = low.split("categories that do not process personal data")[-1]
    if cat in yes_part:
        return Result(TRUE, 1.0, f"'{cat}' is listed as processing personal data",
                      [doc])
    if cat in no_part:
        return Result(FALSE, 1.0,
                      f"'{cat}' is listed as not processing personal data", [doc])
    return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, f"'{cat}' is not classified in the taxonomy", [doc],
                  f"category '{cat}' has no data classification")


def op_dpa_on_file(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    vendor = ctx.get("vendor")
    if not vendor:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "no vendor named", [],
                      "the request does not name a vendor")
    for e in ex:
        if e.doc_id.startswith("contracts/") and vendor.lower() in (
                e.text + " " + e.title).lower():  # noqa: E501
            low = e.text.lower()
            if "no personal data" in low:
                return Result(TRUE, 1.0,
                              "the agreement states no personal data is processed, "
                              "so no addendum is required", [e])
            if "data processing addendum executed" in low:
                dm = ISO.search(e.text[e.text.lower().find("addendum"):])
                return Result(TRUE, 1.0,
                              f"a data processing addendum is executed"
                              f"{' ' + dm.group(1) if dm else ''}", [e])
    reg = pick(ex, "registry/vendors", ctx["as_of"])
    if reg and (line := line_for(reg.text, vendor)) and "dpa on file" in line.lower():
        return Result(TRUE, 1.0, "vendor register records a DPA on file", [reg])
    return Result(FALSE, 0.7, f"no executed DPA found for {vendor}", [],
                  f"no DPA or addendum located for {vendor}")


def op_event_listed(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    event = ctx.get("event")
    if not event:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "no event named", [], "the request names no event")
    doc = pick(ex, "events/calendar", ctx["as_of"])
    if doc is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "approved events calendar not retrieved", [],
                      "approved events calendar is not available")
    line = line_for(doc.text, event)
    if not line:
        return Result(FALSE, 0.8, f"{event} is not on the approved calendar", [doc])
    ok = "approved" in line.lower() and "not approved" not in line.lower()
    return Result(TRUE if ok else FALSE, 1.0,
                  f"calendar entry: {line}", [doc])


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# judge operators: a bounded judgment over supplied evidence
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _judge_exception(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    """Does any exception pre-approve this request? Needs joint evidence."""
    cat = (ctx.get("category") or "").lower()
    amount = ctx.get("amount") or 0.0
    memo = pick(ex, "memo/conference-exception", ctx["as_of"])
    if memo and ("conference" in cat or "travel" in cat):
        cap = money(memo.text)
        event = ctx.get("event")
        cal = pick(ex, "events/calendar", ctx["as_of"])
        listed = bool(cal and event and (line_for(cal.text, event) or "").lower()
                      .find("approved") >= 0 and "not approved" not in
                      (line_for(cal.text, event) or "").lower())
        hosp = "hospitality" in cat or "entertainment" in cat
        if hosp:
            return Result(FALSE, 0.9,
                          "the memo explicitly excludes hospitality and client "
                          "entertainment", [memo], evaluator="judge")
        if cap and amount <= cap and listed and cal:
            return Result(TRUE, 0.95,
                          f"the memo pre-approves conference travel up to "
                          f"${cap:,.0f} and {event} is on the approved calendar",
                          [memo, cal], evaluator="judge", cost=0.0)
        why = []
        if cap and amount > cap:
            why.append(f"amount ${amount:,.0f} exceeds the ${cap:,.0f} pre-approval cap")
        if not listed:
            why.append(f"{event or 'the event'} is not shown as approved on the calendar")
        return Result(FALSE, 0.9,
                      "the conference exception does not apply: " + "; ".join(why),
                      [memo] + ([cal] if cal else []), evaluator="judge")
    emerg = pick(ex, "memo/emergency-procedure", ctx["as_of"])
    if emerg and ("safety" in cat or ctx.get("safety_critical")):
        if "software" in cat or "service" in cat:
            return Result(FALSE, 0.9,
                          "the emergency procedure excludes software and services",
                          [emerg], evaluator="judge")
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.5,
                      "the emergency procedure may apply but requires confirmation "
                      "that the item is safety-critical equipment", [emerg],
                      evaluator="judge")
    return Result(FALSE, 0.85,
                  "no exception memo in the corpus covers this request", [],
                  evaluator="judge")


def _judge_safety_critical(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    desc = (ctx.get("description") or "") + " " + (ctx.get("category") or "")
    memo = pick(ex, "memo/emergency-procedure", ctx["as_of"])
    cues = ("safety", "hazard", "guard", "shutdown", "ventilation",
            "fire", "protection")
    hit = [c for c in cues if c in desc.lower()]
    if hit and memo:
        return Result(TRUE, 0.8,
                      f"description contains safety cues ({', '.join(hit)}) and the "
                      "emergency procedure is in force", [memo], evaluator="judge")
    return Result(FALSE, 0.75,
                  "the description does not indicate safety-critical equipment",
                  [memo] if memo else [], evaluator="judge")


def _judge_is_service(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    """Is this purchase software or services (excluded from the emergency rule)?"""
    desc = ((ctx.get("description") or "") + " " + (ctx.get("category") or "")).lower()
    cues = [c for c in ("software", "subscription", "licence", "license", "saas",
                        "consulting", "service", "hosting", "support plan")
            if c in desc]
    memo = pick(ex, "memo/emergency-procedure", ctx["as_of"])
    if cues:
        return Result(TRUE, 0.85,
                      f"the request names services/software ({', '.join(cues)})",
                      [memo] if memo else [], evaluator="judge")
    return Result(FALSE, 0.8,
                  "the request describes goods, not software or services",
                  [memo] if memo else [], evaluator="judge")


def _judge_supports(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    claim_terms = (p.params.get("claim") or "").lower().split()
    if not ex:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, "no evidence retrieved", [],
                      "no excerpt was returned for this claim", evaluator="judge")
    best = max(ex, key=lambda e: sum(t in e.text.lower() for t in claim_terms))
    cover = sum(t in best.text.lower() for t in claim_terms) / max(1, len(claim_terms))
    if cover >= 0.6:
        return Result(TRUE, round(cover, 2),
                      f"the excerpt covers {cover:.0%} of the claim terms", [best],
                      evaluator="judge")
    return Result(UNKNOWN, round(cover, 2),
                  f"the excerpt covers only {cover:.0%} of the claim terms", [best],
                  "evidence does not settle this claim", evaluator="judge")


def op_fact(p: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt]) -> Result:
    key = p.params.get("key", "")
    val = ctx.get(key)
    if val is None:
        return Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, f"the request does not state {key}", [],
                      f"the request does not state {key}")
    ok = bool(val)
    return Result(TRUE if ok else FALSE, 1.0,
                  f"the request states {key} = {val}", [])


EXACT_OPS = {
    "fact": op_fact,
    "manager_limit": op_manager_limit,
    "vendor_status": op_vendor_status,
    "security_review": op_security_review,
    "budget_line": op_budget_line,
    "personal_data": op_personal_data,
    "dpa_on_file": op_dpa_on_file,
    "event_listed": op_event_listed,
}

JUDGE_OPS = {
    "judge_exception": _judge_exception,
    "judge_is_service": _judge_is_service,
    "judge_safety_critical": _judge_safety_critical,
    "judge_supports": _judge_supports,
}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# evaluators
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

class ReferenceJudge:
    """Offline evaluator for both roles. Deterministic and inspectable."""
    name = "reference"
    is_model = False

    def evaluate(self, pred: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt],
                 corpus=None) -> Result:
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        fn = EXACT_OPS.get(pred.check)
        if fn:
            r = fn(pred, ctx, ex)
        else:
            jf = JUDGE_OPS.get(pred.check)
            if jf is None:
                r = Result(UNKNOWN, 0.0, f"no operator named {pred.check!r}", [],
                           f"unknown operator {pred.check}")
            else:
                r = jf(pred, ctx, ex)
                r.cost = 0.0        # offline reference judge: no model call
        r.ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000
        return r


class LLMJudge(ReferenceJudge):
    """Same interface, model-backed. Used when JIG_LLM_BASE is configured.

    Exact operators still run in code; only judge-kind predicates are sent to the
    model, with the retrieved excerpts as the entire evidence set.
    """
    name = "llm"
    is_model = True

    def __init__(self):
        self.base = os.environ.get("JIG_LLM_BASE", "").rstrip("/")
        self.key = os.environ.get("JIG_LLM_KEY", "")
        self.model = os.environ.get("JIG_LLM_MODEL", "qwen")
        self.calls = 0

    @property
    def available(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.base)

    def evaluate(self, pred: Predicate, ctx: dict, ex: list[Excerpt], corpus=None):
        fn = EXACT_OPS.get(pred.check)
        if fn or not self.available:
            return super().evaluate(pred, ctx, ex, corpus)
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        evidence = "\n\n".join(f"[{e.doc_id}] {e.text}" for e in ex) or "(none)"
        prompt = (
            "You are a bounded evidence judge. Answer only from the evidence.\n"
            f"QUESTION FOR THIS PREDICATE: {pred.ask}\n"
            f"REQUEST FACTS: {json.dumps(ctx, default=str)}\n"
            f"EVIDENCE:\n{evidence}\n"
            'Reply JSON: {"answer":"yes|no|unknown","confidence":0-1,'
            '"rationale":"<=40 words","doc_ids":["..."]}'
        )
        body = json.dumps({"model": self.model, "temperature": 0,
                           "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}]}).encode()
        req = urllib.request.Request(
            self.base + "/chat/completions", data=body,
            headers={"Content-Type": "application/json",
                     "Authorization": f"Bearer {self.key}"})
        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=60) as resp:
            out = json.loads(resp.read())
        self.calls += 1
        txt = out["choices"][0]["message"]["content"]
        m = re.search(r"\{.*\}", txt, re.S)
        parsed = json.loads(m.group(0)) if m else {}
        answer = parsed.get("answer", UNKNOWN)
        if answer not in (TRUE, FALSE, UNKNOWN):
            answer = UNKNOWN
        ids = set(parsed.get("doc_ids") or [])
        cites = [e for e in ex if e.doc_id in ids] or ex[:1]
        r = Result(answer, float(parsed.get("confidence", 0.6)),
                   parsed.get("rationale", "model judgment"),
                   cites, evaluator="judge")
        r.ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000
        return r


def get_judge() -> ReferenceJudge:
    j = LLMJudge()
    return j if j.available else ReferenceJudge()

FILE: programs.py

"""
jig.programs — the decision library: programs, versions, proposals, and the
labelled test set used to judge them.

A program is the thing worth accumulating. It holds the predicates, the source
scope each one may read, the missing-knowledge policy, the combining rule, and
the tests that justified admitting it. Reusing it on a new question is the
operation the whole experiment is about.

PROC-01 ships in two versions on purpose:

  v1.0  authored from the v1.4 policy: limit, vendor, budget, exception.
  v2.0  v1.0 plus the personal-data and security-review predicates that policy
        v2.0 introduced.

v1.0 is what a program looks like before anyone noticed the rule changed. It
gets the Nimbus question wrong. The disagreement is what proposes P3/P4 — the
loop the design calls "use the frontier model to improve the program between
runs". Whether v2.0 is admitted is decided by the intervention tests, not by the
proposal sounding sensible.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from .core import (AND, NOT, OR, P, Predicate, Program, TRUE, FALSE, UNKNOWN,
                   build_cost)

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# threshold that decides whether the planner will answer at all
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

ROUTE_MIN = 1


def _pred(**k) -> Predicate:
    return Predicate(**k)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# PROC-01 — may this purchase be approved without director review?
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

def proc_predicates(with_security: bool) -> list[Predicate]:
    preds = [
        _pred(id="P1", kind="exact", check="manager_limit",
              ask="Is the total value within the manager approval limit in force "
                  "on the purchase date?",
              requires=["policy/"],
              on_missing=UNKNOWN,
              note="exact: threshold lookup in the policy version that governs "
                   "the purchase date"),
        _pred(id="P2", kind="exact", check="vendor_status",
              ask="Is the vendor listed as approved in the vendor register on the "
                  "purchase date?",
              requires=["registry/vendors"],
              on_missing=UNKNOWN, applies_if="has_vendor",
              note="exact: register status"),
        _pred(id="P5", kind="exact", check="budget_line",
              ask="Does the charged budget line have enough remaining and is it "
                  "open?",
              requires=["registry/budget"],
              on_missing=UNKNOWN, applies_if="has_budget_line",
              note="exact: remaining balance and line status"),
        _pred(id="P6", kind="exception", check="judge_exception",
              ask="Does any exception in the corpus pre-approve this request "
                  "(conference travel on the approved calendar, emergency "
                  "procedure)?",
              requires=["memo/", "events/calendar"],
              on_missing=FALSE,
              note="judge: joint evidence from memo + calendar"),
    ]
    if with_security:
        preds.insert(2, _pred(id="P3", kind="exact", check="personal_data",
                              ask="Does this purchase category process personal "
                                  "data?",
                              requires=["policy/data-taxonomy"],
                              on_missing=UNKNOWN, applies_if="has_vendor",
                              note="exact: data classification"))
        preds.insert(3, _pred(id="P4", kind="exact", check="security_review",
                              ask="Is the vendor's security review current within "
                                  "the preceding 12 months?",
                              requires=["registry/security"],
                              on_missing=UNKNOWN, applies_if="has_vendor",
                              note="exact: review register date arithmetic"))
    return preds


PROC_V1 = Program(
    id="PROC-01", family="procurement", title="Purchase approval without director review",
    version="1.0",
    question_template=(
        "Can we approve a ${amount} {category} purchase from {vendor} on "
        "{as_of}, charged to {budget_line}?"),
    slots={"amount": "total value in USD", "category": "spend category",
           "vendor": "supplier", "budget_line": "charged budget code",
           "as_of": "purchase date"},
    criteria="Company procurement policy; manager-level approval only.",
    as_of="purchase date stated in the request",
    predicates=proc_predicates(False),
    combine="P6 OR (P1 AND P2 AND P5)",
    yes_label="APPROVE", no_label="DENY", unknown_label="DEFER",
    route_keywords=["approve", "purchase", "buy", "subscription", "order",
                    "expense", "renew", "charged to"],
    route_requires=["amount"],
    build_tokens_in=68_000, build_tokens_out=9_000,
)

PROC_V2 = Program(
    id="PROC-01", family="procurement", title="Purchase approval without director review",
    version="2.0",
    question_template=PROC_V1.question_template,
    slots=PROC_V1.slots,
    criteria="Company procurement policy v2.0, including the vendor register and "
             "security review conditions.",
    as_of=PROC_V1.as_of,
    predicates=proc_predicates(True),
    combine="P6 OR (P1 AND P2 AND (NOT P3 OR P4) AND P5)",
    yes_label="APPROVE", no_label="DENY", unknown_label="DEFER",
    route_keywords=PROC_V1.route_keywords,
    route_requires=PROC_V1.route_requires,
    authored_by="v1.0 + proposal from disagreement (frontier, verification role)",
    build_tokens_in=18_500, build_tokens_out=3_200,
    lineage=["PROC-01@1.0"],
)

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

DPA_V1 = Program(

        _pred(id="P1", kind="exact", check="personal_data",

EMERG_V1 = Program(
